The working thread

Friday 25 September 2026, 18:03 (PDT), for 2 h 26 min. 207 steps, 77 images looked at.

This is the student's own thread: the brief it was given, what it wrote, each step it took, and every image it opened, shown as it saw it (possibly downscaled). Some of these images were later overwritten in the study folder, so this is the only place they survive. The raw record is the transcript.

The brief

Learn to paint like Mark Ferrari, the great painterly pixel artist, by first understanding his pictures completely. Your task is in ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/shared/TASK.md, with his tarn painting beside it.

Method of study: analysis before any painting. Don't paint the task until the analysis is done.

  1. Analyze the reference rigorously, and draw the analysis as images (with a program, then look at each with the Read tool): - the picture's planes and big masses; - the value structure: a few-value notan, a greyscale reduction, value groups per plane; - the light: direction, what's lit and what's in shadow, where the core shadows and cast shadows fall; - the form of each mass: how the grass banks, rocks and peaks are turned in space; - the edges: hard and soft, lost and found; - the cluster shapes: the characteristic pixel clusters for grass, rock, water and foliage, isolated and magnified; - the palette: extracted, and organized into ramps per material and per light; - how depth is made: overlap, value, hue and detail with distance.
  2. Explain what you've found in words, as a painter would teach it: why each choice works.
  3. Only then construct a method: a program that paints the task's subject (a boulder in a grass bank at a tarn) from parameters, following your analysis. Output the parameter sheets the task asks for.
  4. Check your sheets with the same analyses you applied to Ferrari (notan, values, clusters) and fix what doesn't match.

Research as you go: study more of Ferrari's work (his colour-cycling scenes), and landscape composition and value theory.

Write LESSONS.md: the annotated analysis, the explanation, the method, and an honest analysis-by-analysis comparison of your sheets against the reference.

Rules: work only in ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis/ (read shared/). Don't read or search anywhere else on this disk. Run programs with nice -n 10, at most 4 threads. Don't worry about performance. Take the time mastery takes.

# The task (identical for every study)

Paint, as painterly pixel art in the manner of Mark Ferrari (see ferrari_alpine_tarn_dusk.png beside this file):

**A granite boulder resting in a grass bank at the edge of a still mountain tarn, at dusk.**

- Boulder: 1–3 m, rounded by weathering, partly sunk in the bank. Grass and sedge grow up around its foot.
- The bank slopes gently down to the water's edge. The water reflects the boulder, bank and sky.
- Light: low sun from the left, warm; the sky is pink to violet.
- Frame: 320×180 native pixels, shown upscaled 3× with nearest neighbour. Palette of at most 32 colours, chosen deliberately.
- Camera: a low angle, about 10° down, a few metres away.

The deliverable is a METHOD, not one image. Write a program that paints this subject from parameters, and output SHEETS that sweep them:

- boulder size (small / medium / large);
- light direction (left, front, right, back-lit);
- distance (close, 10 m, 40 m);
- random seed.

Every cell should look painted.
Read: pedagogy/shared/ferrari_alpine_tarn_dusk.png

Bash: Create utilities and magnified grass crops
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/util.py <<'EOF'
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
import os
HERE=os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
ROOT=os.path.dirname(HERE)
def load_ref():
    return np.array(Image.open(os.path.join(ROOT,'img','00_native.png')).convert('RGB'))
def up(a,s): return np.repeat(np.repeat(a,s,0),s,1)
def save(a,name,s=1):
    if a.dtype!=np.uint8: a=np.clip(a,0,255).astype(np.uint8)
    if a.ndim==2: a=np.stack([a]*3,-1)
    Image.fromarray(up(a,s)).save(os.path.join(ROOT,'img',name))
def srgb2lin(c):
    c=np.asarray(c,float)/255; return np.where(c<=0.04045,c/12.92,((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4)
def lum(a):
    """relative luminance -> CIE L* (0..100)"""
    l=srgb2lin(a); Y=l[...,0]*0.2126+l[...,1]*0.7152+l[...,2]*0.0722
    return np.where(Y>0.008856,116*np.cbrt(Y)-16,903.3*Y)
def rgb2lab(a):
    l=srgb2lin(a)
    M=np.array([[0.4124,0.3576,0.1805],[0.2126,0.7152,0.0722],[0.0193,0.1192,0.9505]])
    xyz=l@M.T/np.array([0.95047,1.0,1.08883])
    f=np.where(xyz>0.008856,np.cbrt(xyz),7.787*xyz+16/116)
    L=116*f[...,1]-16; A=500*(f[...,0]-f[...,1]); B=200*(f[...,1]-f[...,2])
    return np.stack([L,A,B],-1)
def label(img,text,pos=(4,4),fill=(255,255,255),bg=(0,0,0)):
    im=Image.fromarray(img) if isinstance(img,np.ndarray) else img
    d=ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    try: f=ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc',14)
    except: f=ImageFont.load_default()
    bb=d.textbbox(pos,text,font=f); d.rectangle([bb[0]-2,bb[1]-2,bb[2]+2,bb[3]+2],fill=bg); d.text(pos,text,fill=fill,font=f)
    return np.array(im)
EOF

import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
a=load_ref(); s=2; g=up(a,s).copy()
im=Image.fromarray(g); d=ImageDraw.Draw(im)
f=ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc',11)
for x in range(0,a.shape[1],20):
    d.line([(x*s,0),(x*s,g.shape[0])],fill=(0,255,255) if x%100==0 else (0,120,120),width=1)
    if x%40==0: d.text((x*s+2,2),str(x),fill=(255,255,0),font=f)
for y in range(0,a.shape[0],20):
    d.line([(0,y*s),(g.shape[1],y*s)],fill=(0,255,255) if y%100==0 else (0,120,120),width=1)
    d.text((2,y*s+2),str(y),fill=(255,255,0),font=f)
im.save('img/01_grid.png')

Bash: Try k-means segmentation of masses
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/seg_try.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
a=load_ref(); lab=rgb2lab(a)
bl=np.stack([gaussian_filter(lab[...,i],3) for i in range(3)],-1)
H,W=a.shape[:2]; yy,xx=np.mgrid[0:H,0:W]
F=np.concatenate([bl.reshape(-1,3),(yy.reshape(-1,1)*0.12),(xx.reshape(-1,1)*0.03)],1)
km=KMeans(16,n_init=4,random_state=0).fit(F[::7]); lab_=km.predict(F).reshape(H,W)
rng=np.random.default_rng(1); cols=rng.integers(40,255,(16,3))
out=cols[lab_].astype(np.uint8)
im=Image.fromarray(up(out,2)); d=ImageDraw.Draw(im); f=ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc',16)
for k in range(16):
    ys,xs=np.nonzero(lab_==k); d.text((int(np.median(xs))*2,int(np.median(ys))*2),str(k),fill=(0,0,0),font=f)
im.save('img/tmp_kmeans.png'); np.save('out/km.npy',lab_)
EOF
nice -n 10 python3 src/seg_try.py

"""Hand-traced planes of the reference (native 634x469 coords) + colour-based sky/tree split.
Output: out/planes.npy (int labels), img/02_planes.png"""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
a=load_ref(); H,W=a.shape[:2]; lab=rgb2lab(a); L=lab[...,0]
NAMES=['sky','mountain','far forest','framing trees','far banks','island','water','left meadow (mid)','left bank (fore)','right bank (fore)']
COLS=[(214,170,196),(150,110,160),(90,80,50),(40,40,20),(120,120,60),(180,160,70),(120,150,210),(230,180,90),(200,120,50),(60,70,30)]
def poly(pts):
    im=Image.new('L',(W,H),0); ImageDraw.Draw(im).polygon(pts,fill=1); return np.array(im)>0
mountain=poly([(150,135),(205,108),(250,100),(285,90),(307,66),(330,84),(355,95),(395,118),(470,128),(505,135),(505,235),(150,235)])
water=poly([(205,250),(560,250),(634,262),(634,285),(560,290),(520,300),(490,320),(450,345),(420,370),(390,395),(360,420),(345,448),(250,448),(235,420),(215,390),(190,360),(165,335),(140,318),(160,300),(205,288)])
island=poly([(257,285),(275,268),(310,258),(360,257),(400,265),(418,285),(400,298),(330,300),(270,296)])
leftmeadow=poly([(0,238),(60,240),(130,248),(205,258),(207,288),(160,300),(140,318),(0,318)])
leftfore=poly([(0,318),(140,318),(165,335),(190,360),(215,390),(235,420),(250,448),(262,469),(0,469)])
rightfore=poly([(634,285),(560,290),(520,300),(490,320),(450,345),(420,370),(390,395),(360,420),(345,448),(330,469),(634,469)])
farbanks=poly([(80,212),(200,215),(300,225),(420,222),(500,215),(634,215),(634,262),(560,250),(205,250),(205,258),(130,248),(60,240),(0,238),(0,225)])
framing=poly([(0,0),(175,0),(175,245),(0,245)])|poly([(490,0),(634,0),(634,235),(490,235)])
skyish=(L>50)&(lab[...,1]>3)&(lab[...,2]<30)   # pink/violet high value
yy,xx=np.mgrid[0:H,0:W]
lab_=np.full((H,W),2)                      # default far forest (upper middle)
lab_[framing]=3
lab_[mountain & ~framing]=1
# inside mountain zone, conifer colours stay forest: dark or yellow-ochre
coniferish=(L<35)|((lab[...,2]>25)&(lab[...,1]<25))
lab_[mountain & ~framing & coniferish]=2
lab_[(yy<245)&skyish&~mountain]=0
lab_[(yy<140)&skyish]=0
lab_[farbanks]=4; lab_[water]=6; lab_[island]=5
lab_[leftmeadow]=7; lab_[leftfore]=8; lab_[rightfore]=9
np.save('out/planes.npy',lab_)
C=np.array(COLS,np.uint8); out=C[lab_]
mix=(0.45*a+0.55*out).astype(np.uint8)
img=up(mix,2)
im=Image.fromarray(img); d=ImageDraw.Draw(im); f=ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc',15)
for i,n in enumerate(NAMES):
    d.rectangle([8,8+i*20,24,22+i*20],fill=COLS[i],outline=(0,0,0)); d.text((30,8+i*20),f'{i} {n}',fill=(255,255,255),font=f,stroke_width=2,stroke_fill=(0,0,0))
im.save('img/02_planes.png')
for i,n in enumerate(NAMES): print(i,n,(lab_==i).mean().round(3))

Bash: Compute greyscale, notan and per-plane values
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/values.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg'); import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
def analyse(a,planes,names,tag,prefix):
    L=lum(a); Lb=gaussian_filter(L,2.5)
    g=(L/100*255); gb=(Lb/100*255)
    save(np.concatenate([g,np.full((a.shape[0],6),255),gb],1),f'{prefix}_grey.png',2 if a.shape[1]>400 else 3)
    res={}
    rows=[]
    for k in (2,3,5):
        km=KMeans(k,n_init=6,random_state=0).fit(Lb.reshape(-1,1)[::5]); c=np.sort(km.cluster_centers_.ravel())
        th=(c[1:]+c[:-1])/2; q=np.digitize(Lb,th); lv=np.linspace(0,255,k) if k>2 else np.array([30,235])
        if k==3: lv=np.array([25,130,240])
        rows.append(lv[q]); res[k]=(th,c)
        np.save(f'out/{prefix}_notan{k}.npy',q)
    sep=np.full((a.shape[0],6),255)
    save(np.concatenate([rows[0],sep,rows[1],sep,rows[2]],1),f'{prefix}_notan.png',1 if a.shape[1]>400 else 2)
    # per-plane stats
    stats=[]
    for i,n in enumerate(names):
        m=planes==i
        if m.sum()<50: stats.append(None); continue
        stats.append((n,np.percentile(Lb[m],[5,25,50,75,95]),L[m].std(),m.mean()))
    fig,ax=plt.subplots(1,2,figsize=(13,4.6))
    data=[Lb[planes==i] for i in range(len(names)) if (planes==i).sum()>=50]
    nm=[n for i,n in enumerate(names) if (planes==i).sum()>=50]
    ax[0].boxplot(data,whis=(5,95),showfliers=False,vert=False); ax[0].set_yticks(range(1,len(nm)+1),nm); ax[0].set_xlabel('L* (squint-blurred)'); ax[0].set_xlim(0,100); ax[0].set_title(f'{tag}: value range per plane')
    for t in res[3][0]: ax[0].axvline(t,color='r',ls=':')
    ax[1].hist(L.ravel(),bins=100,range=(0,100),color='k',alpha=.5,label='pixel L*'); ax[1].hist(Lb.ravel(),bins=100,range=(0,100),color='r',alpha=.4,label='blurred L*'); ax[1].legend(); ax[1].set_title('value histogram (red dotted = 3-notan cuts)')
    for t in res[3][0]: ax[1].axvline(t,color='r',ls=':')
    plt.tight_layout(); plt.savefig(f'img/{prefix}_valueplanes.png',dpi=80); plt.close()
    # flat value plan: each plane filled with its median blurred L
    flat=np.zeros_like(L)
    for i in range(len(names)):
        m=planes==i
        if m.any(): flat[m]=np.median(Lb[m])
    save(flat/100*255,f'{prefix}_valueplan.png',1 if a.shape[1]>400 else 2)
    return res,stats
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); planes=np.load('out/planes.npy')
    from planes_names import NAMES
    res,stats=analyse(a,planes,NAMES,'Ferrari','03')
    for k,(th,c) in res.items(): print(k,'centers',c.round(1),'cuts',th.round(1))
    for s in stats:
        if s: print(f'{s[0]:20s} p5/25/50/75/95={s[1].round(0)} pixstd={s[2]:.1f} area={s[3]:.3f}')
EOF

Bash: Probe sky gradient and lit-side statistics
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/light_probe.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy'); lab=rgb2lab(a); L=lab[...,0]
sky=P==0
# sky gradient: by row and by column
for y in range(0,240,20):
    m=sky[y:y+10]; 
    if m.sum(): print('row',y,'L=%.1f a=%.1f b=%.1f'%tuple(lab[y:y+10][m].mean(0)))
H,W=L.shape
for x0 in range(0,W,80):
    m=sky[60:200,x0:x0+80]
    if m.sum()>100: print('col',x0,'L=%.1f a=%.1f b=%.1f'%tuple(lab[60:200,x0:x0+80][m].mean(0)))
# which side of dark are the warm lit pixels, per plane
for pi,name in [(3,'framing trees'),(2,'far forest'),(1,'mountain'),(4,'far banks'),(5,'island')]:
    m=P==pi; lit=m&(L>np.percentile(L[m],70)); dark=L<np.percentile(L[m],40)
    left=np.zeros_like(lit); right=np.zeros_like(lit); upn=np.zeros_like(lit); dn=np.zeros_like(lit)
    # for lit pixels, is the dark neighbour at distance 1..3 to the right (=> lit on left side of form)?
    r=l=u=d=0
    for k in (1,2,3):
        r+=(lit[:,:-k]&dark[:,k:]).sum(); l+=(lit[:,k:]&dark[:,:-k]).sum()
        d+=(lit[:-k]&dark[k:]).sum(); u+=(lit[k:]&dark[:-k]).sum()
    print(f'{name:14s} lit->dark: right {r} left {l} below {d} above {u}  => lit edge faces {"LEFT" if r>l else "RIGHT"}, {"UP" if d>u else "DOWN"}')
# water vs source brightness: reflected sky
w=P==6
print('water pixels L>55 mean', L[w&(L>55)].mean(), ' sky mean', L[sky].mean())
EOF

Bash: Measure reflection axis and value shift
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/reflection.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
a=load_ref(); lab=rgb2lab(a); L=lab[...,0]; Lb=gaussian_filter(L,1.5)
# find mirror axis y0 (possibly half-integer) and vertical scale s: water row y maps to source row y0-(y-y0)*s
best=None
xs=slice(250,420)
for y0 in np.arange(225,262,0.5):
  for s in (0.8,0.85,0.9,0.95,1.0,1.05,1.1):
    ys=np.arange(300,400); src=np.round(y0-(ys-y0)*s).astype(int)
    if src.min()<0: continue
    e=np.mean(np.abs(Lb[ys][:,xs]-Lb[src][:,xs]))
    if best is None or e<best[0]: best=(e,y0,s)
print('best axis',best)
e,y0,s=best
ys=np.arange(300,400); src=np.round(y0-(ys-y0)*s).astype(int)
dL=Lb[ys][:,xs]-Lb[src][:,xs]
print('mean dL refl-src %.2f'%dL.mean())
for lo,hi in [(0,30),(30,55),(55,100)]:
    m=(Lb[src][:,xs]>=lo)&(Lb[src][:,xs]<hi); print(f' src L {lo}-{hi}: refl-src dL={dL[m].mean():.2f}  (n={m.sum()})')
labb=np.stack([gaussian_filter(lab[...,i],1.5) for i in range(3)],-1)
d=labb[ys][:,xs]-labb[src][:,xs]; print('mean dLab',d.reshape(-1,3).mean(0).round(2))
# side by side image: source (flipped) vs reflection
flip=a[src][:,250:420]; refl=a[ys][:,250:420]
save(np.concatenate([flip,np.full((100,4,3),255,np.uint8),refl],1),'06_reflection_compare.png',4)
EOF

Bash: Draw isophotes and brightness-gradient arrows per mass
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/form.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from skimage import measure
a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy'); L=lum(a)
s=3; img=Image.fromarray(up((0.55*a+0.45*np.stack([L*2.55]*3,-1)).astype(np.uint8),s)); d=ImageDraw.Draw(img)
Lb=gaussian_filter(L,4); gy,gx=np.gradient(gaussian_filter(L,7))
cols={1:(255,120,255),4:(120,255,120),5:(255,255,0),7:(255,160,0),8:(255,80,0),9:(0,255,255),6:(160,160,255)}
for pi,c in cols.items():
    m=P==pi
    Lm=np.where(m,Lb,np.nan)
    # isophotes of the squinted value within the mass
    lv=np.nanpercentile(Lm,[20,40,60,80])
    for v in lv:
        for cn in measure.find_contours(np.nan_to_num(Lm,nan=-99),v):
            pts=[(p[1]*s,p[0]*s) for p in cn[::2]]
            if len(pts)>6: d.line(pts,fill=c,width=1)
    # brightness gradient arrows (points toward the lit side of the form)
    for y in range(8,a.shape[0],18):
        for x in range(8,a.shape[1],18):
            if m[y,x] and m[max(0,y-6):y+6,max(0,x-6):x+6].mean()>0.9:
                vx,vy=gx[y,x],gy[y,x]; n=np.hypot(vx,vy)
                if n<0.15: d.ellipse([x*s-2,y*s-2,x*s+2,y*s+2],outline=c); continue
                k=min(9,3+n*6)/n; x2,y2=x+vx*k,y+vy*k
                d.line([(x*s,y*s),(x2*s,y2*s)],fill=c,width=2); d.ellipse([x2*s-3,y2*s-3,x2*s+3,y2*s+3],fill=c)
img.save('img/07_form.png')
img.crop((180*s,190*s,634*s,330*s)).save('img/07_form_mid.png')
EOF

Bash: Classify hard/soft edges and measure boundary contrast
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/edges.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, gaussian_gradient_magnitude, binary_dilation, median_filter
def edge_analysis(a,prefix,scale):
    L=lum(a)
    # suppress dither: median 3x3 first (dither checker -> flat), then scale-space gradient
    Lm=median_filter(L,3)
    g1=gaussian_gradient_magnitude(Lm,1.0); g3=gaussian_gradient_magnitude(Lm,3.0)
    strong=g3>np.percentile(g3,80)
    hard=g1/(g3+1e-6)
    base=np.stack([L*2.0+40]*3,-1).clip(0,255)
    out=base.copy()
    H=strong&(hard>1.9); S=strong&(hard<=1.9)
    out[H]=(255,40,40); out[S]=(60,140,255)
    save(out.astype(np.uint8),f'{prefix}_edges.png',scale)
    return H.sum()/strong.sum(), g3, hard, strong
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy')
    fr,g3,hard,strong=edge_analysis(a,'08',2)
    print('share of strong edges that are hard: %.2f'%fr)
    L=gaussian_filter(lum(a),2)
    from planes_names import NAMES
    # plane-boundary contrast: found vs lost
    res={}
    for i in range(10):
        for j in range(i+1,10):
            bi=binary_dilation(P==i,iterations=2)&(P==j)
            if bi.sum()<60: continue
            # value 4px inside each side
            mi=binary_dilation(P==j,iterations=4)&(P==i); mj=binary_dilation(P==i,iterations=4)&(P==j)
            d=abs(np.median(L[mi])-np.median(L[mj])); res[(i,j)]=d
            print(f'{NAMES[i]:18s}|{NAMES[j]:18s} dL={d:5.1f} hardfrac={(hard[bi&strong]>1.9).mean() if (bi&strong).any() else 0:.2f} len={bi.sum()}')
EOF
nice -n 10 python3 src/edges.py

sky               |far forest         dL= 34.2 hardfrac=0.63 len=1657
sky               |framing trees      dL= 36.4 hardfrac=0.66 len=8183

Bash: Measure edge rise widths along gradient normals
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/edges.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, median_filter, map_coordinates, binary_dilation
from skimage.feature import canny
def edge_widths(a):
    L=lum(a); Lm=gaussian_filter(median_filter(L,3),0.7)
    E=canny(median_filter(L,3),sigma=2.0,low_threshold=3,high_threshold=6)
    gy,gx=np.gradient(gaussian_filter(Lm,2.0))
    ys,xs=np.nonzero(E); n=np.hypot(gx[ys,xs],gy[ys,xs])+1e-9; ux,uy=gx[ys,xs]/n,gy[ys,xs]/n
    t=np.linspace(-8,8,33)
    py=ys[:,None]+uy[:,None]*t[None]; px=xs[:,None]+ux[:,None]*t[None]
    prof=map_coordinates(Lm,[py,px],order=1,mode='nearest')
    lo=np.percentile(prof,10,axis=1); hi=np.percentile(prof,90,axis=1); amp=hi-lo
    p=(prof-lo[:,None])/(amp[:,None]+1e-9)
    # rise width: distance between where profile crosses .2 and .8 nearest centre
    w=np.zeros(len(ys))
    for i in range(len(ys)):
        c=16; q=p[i]
        a20=np.argmin(np.where(np.abs(np.arange(33)-c)<=16,np.abs(q-0.2),9)); a80=np.argmin(np.abs(q-0.8))
        w[i]=abs(t[a80]-t[a20])
    return ys,xs,w,amp,L
def draw(a,prefix,scale):
    ys,xs,w,amp,L=edge_widths(a)
    base=np.stack([L*1.6+60]*3,-1).clip(0,255)
    keep=amp>8
    out=base.copy()
    col=np.zeros((len(ys),3))
    col[w<=2.0]=(230,20,20); col[(w>2.0)&(w<=4.5)]=(250,200,0); col[w>4.5]=(30,120,255)
    out[ys[keep],xs[keep]]=col[keep]
    save(out.astype(np.uint8),f'{prefix}_edges.png',scale)
    return ys[keep],xs[keep],w[keep],amp[keep]
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy')
    from planes_names import NAMES
    ys,xs,w,amp=draw(a,'08',2)
    print('edge px',len(w),'hard(<=2) %.2f mid %.2f soft(>4.5) %.2f'%((w<=2).mean(),((w>2)&(w<=4.5)).mean(),(w>4.5).mean()))
    for i,n in enumerate(NAMES):
        m=P[ys,xs]==i
        if m.sum()>30: print(f'{n:18s} n={m.sum():5d} hard {np.mean(w[m]<=2):.2f} soft {np.mean(w[m]>4.5):.2f} median amp {np.median(amp[m]):.1f}')
EOF

Bash: Compute same-colour cluster statistics per plane
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/clusters.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import label as cclabel, find_objects
def colour_index(a):
    flat=a.reshape(-1,3); u,inv=np.unique(flat,axis=0,return_inverse=True); return inv.reshape(a.shape[:2]),u
def cluster_stats(a,mask):
    """connected same-colour runs (4-conn) inside mask: size, h, w, aspect (h/w), vertical fraction"""
    idx,u=colour_index(a)
    S=[];Hs=[];Ws=[];
    for c in np.unique(idx[mask]):
        m=(idx==c)&mask
        lab,n=cclabel(m)
        if n==0: continue
        sizes=np.bincount(lab.ravel())[1:]
        for sl,sz in zip(find_objects(lab),sizes):
            S.append(sz); Hs.append(sl[0].stop-sl[0].start); Ws.append(sl[1].stop-sl[1].start)
    S=np.array(S);Hs=np.array(Hs);Ws=np.array(Ws)
    return dict(n=len(S),size_med=np.median(S),size_mean=S.mean(),size_p90=np.percentile(S,90),
                single=(S==1).mean(),vert=np.mean(Hs>Ws),horiz=np.mean(Ws>Hs),aspect_med=np.median(Hs/Ws),
                h_p90=np.percentile(Hs,90), w_p90=np.percentile(Ws,90),
                colours=len(np.unique(idx[mask])), per100=len(S)/mask.sum()*100)
def run_lengths(a,mask,axis):
    """mean run length of identical colour along axis inside mask"""
    idx,_=colour_index(a); b=idx if axis==1 else idx.T; mk=mask if axis==1 else mask.T
    runs=[]
    for r,m in zip(b,mk):
        if m.sum()<4: continue
        v=r[m]; ch=np.flatnonzero(np.diff(v)!=0); pts=np.r_[-1,ch,len(v)-1]; runs+=list(np.diff(pts))
    return np.mean(runs)
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy')
    from planes_names import NAMES
    import json; out={}
    for i,n in enumerate(NAMES):
        m=P==i; s=cluster_stats(a,m); s['runH']=run_lengths(a,m,1); s['runV']=run_lengths(a,m,0)
        out[n]={k:float(v) for k,v in s.items()}
        print(f"{n:18s} cols={s['colours']:3.0f} clusters/100px={s['per100']:5.1f} size med={s['size_med']:.0f} mean={s['size_mean']:5.1f} p90={s['size_p90']:4.0f} single={s['single']:.2f} vert={s['vert']:.2f} horiz={s['horiz']:.2f} runH={s['runH']:.2f} runV={s['runV']:.2f}")
    json.dump(out,open('out/ref_cluster_stats.json','w'),indent=1)
EOF

Bash: Build magnified cluster atlas with colour isolation
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/atlas.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from collections import Counter
def atlas(a,wins,name,s=7,sz=32):
    tiles=[]
    for title,(x,y) in wins:
        w=a[y:y+sz,x:x+sz]
        cnt=Counter(map(tuple,w.reshape(-1,3))).most_common(3)
        row=[up(w,s)]
        for col,_ in cnt:   # isolate each of the 3 dominant colours
            m=np.all(w==col,axis=2); t=np.full_like(w,128); t[m]=col; t[~m]=(40,40,40) if np.mean(col)>90 else (200,200,200)
            row.append(up(t,s//2+1)[:sz*s//2+sz//2*0+ sz*(s//2+1)][:,:])
        # compose: big tile + 3 small isolation tiles stacked
        big=row[0]; smalls=[r[:sz*(s//2+1),:sz*(s//2+1)] for r in row[1:]]
        colh=big.shape[0]; sw=smalls[0].shape[1]
        side=np.full((colh,sw,3),255,np.uint8); yy=0
        for sm in smalls:
            h=min(sm.shape[0],colh-yy); side[yy:yy+h]=sm[:h]; yy+=h+2
            if yy>=colh: break
        t=np.concatenate([big,np.full((colh,3,3),255,np.uint8),side],1)
        t=np.concatenate([np.full((22,t.shape[1],3),255,np.uint8),t],0)
        t=label(t,title,(2,3),fill=(0,0,0),bg=(255,255,255))
        tiles.append(t)
    W=max(t.shape[1] for t in tiles); rows=[]
    for i in range(0,len(tiles),3):
        r=tiles[i:i+3]; r=[np.pad(t,((0,0),(0,W-t.shape[1]+6),(0,0)),constant_values=255) for t in r]
        while len(r)<3: r.append(np.full_like(r[0],255))
        rows.append(np.concatenate(r,1))
    Image.fromarray(np.concatenate(rows,0)).save(f'img/{name}')
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref()
    wins=[('fore grass, lit (left)',(40,400)),('fore grass, shade (right)',(560,400)),('mid meadow, lit',(60,262)),
          ('far bank / hummock',(520,228)),('island top (rim light)',(340,258)),('conifer, near',(40,120)),
          ('conifer edge vs sky',(95,60)),('mountain rock + light',(215,110)),('snow, shade/light',(318,120)),
          ('water: sky reflection',(300,370)),('water: tree reflection',(290,310)),('shore stones',(250,405))]
    atlas(a,wins,'09_cluster_atlas.png')
EOF

Bash: Organize palette into material ramps and chart
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/palette.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg'); import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from planes_names import NAMES
a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy')
flat=a.reshape(-1,3); u,inv,cnt=np.unique(flat,axis=0,return_inverse=True,return_counts=True); inv=inv.ravel()
pl=P.ravel()
M=np.zeros((len(u),10))
np.add.at(M,(inv,pl),1)
lab=rgb2lab(u.astype(float)); L=lab[:,0]; hue=np.degrees(np.arctan2(lab[:,2],lab[:,1]))%360; C=np.hypot(lab[:,1],lab[:,2])
# material groups (merge planes)
groups={'sky':[0],'mountain (rock+snow)':[1],'conifers':[2,3],'grass lit (left)':[7,8],'grass shade / banks':[4,9],'island':[5],'water-only':[6]}
home=np.argmax(M,1)
own={}
for g,ps in groups.items():
    share=M[:,ps].sum(1)/M.sum(1)
    if g=='water-only': sel=(M[:,6]/M.sum(1)>0.9)
    else: sel=(share>0.45)&(M[:,6]/M.sum(1)<=0.9)
    own[g]=np.flatnonzero(sel)
# assign each colour to a single group (highest share excl water), ignore duplicates
assigned={}
for i in range(len(u)):
    best=None
    for g,ps in groups.items():
        if g=='water-only': continue
        s=M[i,ps].sum()
        if best is None or s>best[1]: best=(g,s)
    if M[i,6]/M[i].sum()>0.9: best=('water-only',0)
    assigned.setdefault(best[0],[]).append(i)
sw=18; rows=[]
for g in groups:
    ids=sorted(assigned.get(g,[]),key=lambda i:L[i])
    tot=cnt[ids].sum()
    strip=np.full((sw+20,max(1,len(ids))*sw+260,3),255,np.uint8)
    for k,i in enumerate(ids):
        h=int(np.clip(4+np.log1p(cnt[i])*1.5,4,sw))
        strip[20+sw-h:20+sw,260+k*sw:260+(k+1)*sw-1]=u[i]
    strip=label(strip,f'{g} ({len(ids)} cols)',(2,20),fill=(0,0,0),bg=(255,255,255))
    rows.append(strip)
W=max(r.shape[1] for r in rows); rows=[np.pad(r,((0,4),(0,W-r.shape[1]),(0,0)),constant_values=255) for r in rows]
Image.fromarray(np.concatenate(rows,0)).save('img/10_palette_groups.png')
# chart: L vs hue per group, point size by count
fig,ax=plt.subplots(1,2,figsize=(14,5.5))
for g in groups:
    ids=assigned.get(g,[])
    if not ids: continue
    ids=np.array(ids)
    ax[0].scatter(lab[ids,1],lab[ids,2],s=np.sqrt(cnt[ids])*1.2,c=u[ids]/255,edgecolors='k',linewidths=.3)
    ax[1].scatter(C[ids],L[ids],s=np.sqrt(cnt[ids])*1.2,c=u[ids]/255,edgecolors='k',linewidths=.3)
ax[0].set_xlabel('a*  (green - / red +)'); ax[0].set_ylabel('b*  (blue - / yellow +)'); ax[0].axhline(0,c='gray',lw=.5); ax[0].axvline(0,c='gray',lw=.5); ax[0].set_title('all 172 colours in a*b* (size ~ usage)')
ax[1].set_xlabel('chroma'); ax[1].set_ylabel('L*'); ax[1].set_title('value vs chroma')
plt.tight_layout(); plt.savefig('img/10_palette_ab.png',dpi=80)
# coverage: how many colours cover 90% of each plane
for i,n in enumerate(NAMES):
    c=np.sort(M[:,i])[::-1]; cs=np.cumsum(c)/c.sum(); print(f'{n:18s} colours for 80%: {np.searchsorted(cs,.8)+1:3d}  90%: {np.searchsorted(cs,.9)+1:3d}  95%: {np.searchsorted(cs,.95)+1:3d}')
# print the dominant ramps
for g in ['grass lit (left)','grass shade / banks','conifers','mountain (rock+snow)','sky']:
    ids=sorted(assigned[g],key=lambda i:-cnt[i])[:10]; ids=sorted(ids,key=lambda i:L[i])
    print(g,[ (tuple(u[i]),int(L[i]),int(hue[i]),int(C[i])) for i in ids])
EOF

Great! I found relevant information about Mark Ferrari, his color cycling and dithering techniques, and his GDC talk. Here's what I found:

## Mark Ferrari's GDC Talk: "8-Bit & '8 Bitish' Graphics—Outside the Box"

In his GDC 2016 talk, Mark Ferrari discusses and demonstrates his techniques for drawing 8-bit game graphics, including his celebrated methods for color cycling and palette shifting to create complex and realistic background animation effects without frame-animation.

## Dithering Technique

Ferrari is a pioneer of the dithering technique, which he initially developed by using checkerboard dithering to create more useful colors from a limited palette. Games at that time were much more low-res and had only 16 colors to work with, which were 16 pre-selected colors chosen by programmers rather than artists.

## Color Cycling

Color cycling is powered by changing specific colors in a color palette that gives the illusion of animation. Ron Gilbert pointed out a feature of the program Deluxe Paint called color cycling while working on The Secret of Monkey Island, which became instrumental in animating scenes like the bonfire.

## Ferrari's Work

Mark worked on graphics for many well-known Lucasfilm games, including Zak McKracken, Monkey Island, and Loom. His dithering art style won awards for the game 'Loom' and was used in subsequent titles.

I found several relevant results about Mark Ferrari and his work with color cycling, pixel art, and the Living Worlds project. Here's what the search revealed:

## Mark Ferrari's Color Cycling Expertise

Mark J. Ferrari illustrated backgrounds for LucasArts games including Loom and The Secret of Monkey Island, and invented unique ways of using color cycling for environmental effects. These effects include rain, snow, ocean waves, moving fog, clouds, smoke, waterfalls, streams, and lakes—all achieved without layers or alpha channels using just one single flat image with one 256 color palette.

## Understanding Color Cycling

According to Mark Ferrari, color cycling creates the illusion of animated movement similarly to theater marquee lights, with pixels changing colors in a looping sequence to create the illusion of moving bands of color.

## Living Worlds Project

The Living Worlds app revived a collection of beloved, classic pixel art from Mark Ferrari. The project uses color palette tricks like color cycling and palette fading to bring the world alive.

## Canvas Cycle Implementation

Canvas Cycle is an implementation of a full 8-bit color cycling engine rendered into an HTML5 Canvas in real-time, using 35 of Mark's original 640x480 pixel masterpieces.

## Career Impact

Ferrari was one of the only people in the world doing color cycling work, but the advent of 3D CAD systems made that skill irrelevant, and his career ended in 1996 when the industry adopted 3D rendering.

Summarize everything Mark Ferrari says about his technique: how he paints, dithering, palettes, how many colours, how he plans colour cycling ranges, tools (DPaint), approach to light, time of day variants, water, and any advice. Give short paraphrases, and at most one short direct quote.

# result:
# Mark Ferrari's Color Cycling Technique

**Core Method:**
Ferrari creates static pixel art that appears animated through color palette cycling—pixels change colors in coordinated sequences, similar to theater marquee lights flashing in patterns.

**Palette & Colors:**
He works within indexed 256-color palettes, carefully managing palette space. Many palettes contain unused rows reserved for effects not active in that scene state.

**Dithering Strategy:**
Choice varies by artistic need. Patterned dithering produces smooth, precise effects; diffusion dithering creates chaotic textures (like foam); no dither maintains solid, unbroken color bands for rain/snow.

**Cycling Ranges:**
Complex images use multiple gradient lengths and speeds simultaneously—some slow and long, others fast and short—creating organic movement without rigid pulsing. "Gradients of varying length and speed...produce a much smoother, less noticeably syncopated...result."

**Tools:**
Deluxe Paint II was essential—superior at palette management and stencil features. Modern alternative: Pro Motion (with limitations in stencil position-tracking and palette persistence).

**Light & Time Variants:**
Single artwork shifts through multiple palettes to show different times of day and weather. Light and shadows appear to move across surfaces through palette transitions alone, without altering the base pixel art.

**Water Effects:**
Moving reflections use stenciled cycling gradients laid repeatedly at varying color/value intensities.

**Timeframe:**
3 weeks maximum per image (8-14 hours daily), depending on effect complexity.

# result:
# Mark Ferrari on His Artistic Approach

**Dithering Technique:**
Ferrari pioneered dithering as a workaround for severe technical limitations. Working with only 16 pre-selected, poorly chosen colors, he created checkerboard patterns that blurred on CRT monitors to simulate additional hues, essentially "mixing paint" digitally. Initially resisted by programmers concerned about compression, his technique eventually became the studio standard after he created a dithered twilight scene as a silent protest.

**Learning and Palette:**
Ferrari learned through necessity rather than formal training. He arrived at Lucasfilm Games as a self-described technophobe who'd never used a computer. The company's philosophy was enlightening: "They preferred to find good artists and teach them to use a computer instead of teaching a programmer to make art." His expertise came from experimenting within extreme technical constraints and collaborating with talented colleagues like Ron Gilbert and Gary Winnick.

**Impact:**
His mastery of 2D color manipulation made him highly sought after throughout the 1990s, but the industry's shift to 3D rendering in 1996 rendered his distinctive style obsolete overnight, leaving him without work for five years.

Bash: Measure checkerboard dither usage per plane
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/dither.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
def dither_stats(a,P,names):
    i00=a[:-1,:-1];i01=a[:-1,1:];i10=a[1:,:-1];i11=a[1:,1:]
    eq=lambda p,q: np.all(p==q,axis=2)
    checker=eq(i00,i11)&eq(i01,i10)&~eq(i00,i01)
    flat=eq(i00,i01)&eq(i00,i10)&eq(i00,i11)
    vstripe=eq(i00,i10)&eq(i01,i11)&~eq(i00,i01)
    hstripe=eq(i00,i01)&eq(i10,i11)&~eq(i00,i10)
    Pc=P[:-1,:-1]; res={}
    for i,n in enumerate(names):
        m=Pc==i
        if m.sum()<50: continue
        res[n]=dict(checker=checker[m].mean(),flat=flat[m].mean(),vstripe=vstripe[m].mean(),hstripe=hstripe[m].mean())
    return res,checker
if __name__=='__main__':
    from planes_names import NAMES
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy'); res,ch=dither_stats(a,P,NAMES)
    for n,r in res.items(): print(f"{n:18s} "+' '.join(f'{k}={v:.3f}' for k,v in r.items()))
    out=(a*0.3+120).astype(np.uint8); out[:-1,:-1][ch]=(255,0,200); save(out,'11_checker_map.png',2)
EOF

Bash: Compute depth cues per plane and plot
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/depth.py <<'EOF'
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter, laplace
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg'); import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
def depth_stats(a,P,order):
    lab=rgb2lab(a); L=lab[...,0]; C=np.hypot(lab[...,1],lab[...,2])
    Lb=gaussian_filter(L,2.5); hf=np.abs(L-gaussian_filter(L,1.0))
    rows=[]
    for name,ids in order:
        m=np.isin(P,ids)
        rows.append(dict(name=name,L=np.median(Lb[m]),Lrange=np.percentile(Lb[m],95)-np.percentile(Lb[m],5),
            C=np.median(C[m]),b=np.median(lab[...,2][m]),a=np.median(lab[...,1][m]),hf=hf[m].mean(),darkest=np.percentile(L[m],3),lightest=np.percentile(L[m],97)))
    return rows
ORDER=[('near: fore banks',[8,9]),('near: framing trees',[3]),('mid: left meadow',[7]),('mid: island',[5]),('far: banks',[4]),('far: forest',[2]),('distant: mountain',[1]),('sky',[0])]
def plot(rows,fn,title):
    fig,ax=plt.subplots(1,4,figsize=(16,3.6)); x=np.arange(len(rows)); nm=[r['name'] for r in rows]
    ax[0].plot(x,[r['L'] for r in rows],'ko-',label='median L*'); ax[0].fill_between(x,[r['darkest'] for r in rows],[r['lightest'] for r in rows],alpha=.2,label='3-97% pixel L*'); ax[0].set_title('value & value range'); ax[0].legend(fontsize=7)
    ax[1].plot(x,[r['Lrange'] for r in rows],'ro-'); ax[1].set_title('squint contrast (p95-p5)')
    ax[2].plot(x,[r['C'] for r in rows],'go-',label='chroma'); ax[2].plot(x,[r['b'] for r in rows],'y^-',label='b* (warm +)'); ax[2].plot(x,[r['a'] for r in rows],'m^-',label='a* (pink +)'); ax[2].legend(fontsize=7); ax[2].set_title('colour')
    ax[3].plot(x,[r['hf'] for r in rows],'bo-'); ax[3].set_title('detail (hi-freq energy)')
    for axx in ax: axx.set_xticks(x,nm,rotation=60,fontsize=7)
    fig.suptitle(title); plt.tight_layout(); plt.savefig(fn,dpi=80); plt.close()
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy'); rows=depth_stats(a,P,ORDER)
    for r in rows: print({k:(round(v,1) if not isinstance(v,str) else v) for k,v in r.items()})
    plot(rows,'img/12_depth.png','Ferrari: depth cues, near -> far')
EOF

Write: pedagogy/analysis/src/paint/ferrari_method.py
"""
A boulder in a grass bank at the edge of a still tarn, at dusk -- painted as Ferrari paints.

The method is the analysis turned into procedure (see LESSONS.md):

  1. geometry  -> a G-buffer: what material is at each pixel, how far, which way it faces
  2. light     -> a target VALUE per pixel (value first), and whether it is in light or shadow
  3. colour    -> the value is looked up in the MATERIAL's ramp: warm ramp in light, cool ramp in
                  shadow (colour second). 32 colours, fixed, chosen per material.
  4. clusters  -> the in-between values are made with the material's own pixel-cluster pattern:
                  sky = flat bands with checker seams, grass = vertical strokes, rock = diagonal
                  facet strokes and crest lines, water = horizontal ripples, far meadow = speckle.
  5. strokes   -> grass blades and sedge painted on top, back to front, in counterchange against
                  what lies behind them; they break the boulder's foot and the waterline.
  6. reflection-> the water mirrors everything, with lights pulled down and cooled, darks kept,
                  laid with horizontal ripple dither and a few streak lines.

Parameters: size (small/medium/large), light (left/front/right/back), distance (close/10m/40m), seed.
"""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

W, H = 320, 180
HFOV = np.radians(50.0)
F = (W / 2) / np.tan(HFOV / 2)            # focal length in pixels (~343)
PITCH = np.radians(10.0)                  # camera looks 10 degrees down
HC = 1.1                                  # camera height above the water (m)
Y_H = H / 2 - F * np.tan(PITCH)           # horizon row (~29.5)

# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PALETTE: 32 colours, organised as material ramps (sorted dark -> light inside each ramp).
# Values follow the reference: sky ~L70-78, distant rock lifted to >=L37, grass ramps spaced
# ~12-18 L apart, near darks down to black.  Lit ramps are warm (ochre / peach), shadow ramps cool
# (violet) or dull olive.
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PAL = {
    # sky: nearly iso-luminant hue sweep violet -> peach
    'sky0': (190, 158, 200), 'sky1': (206, 162, 188), 'sky2': (222, 162, 174),
    'sky3': (236, 168, 160), 'sky4': (246, 188, 160), 'hot': (252, 212, 158),
    # distant mountain: darks lifted by atmosphere
    'mtn0': (98, 80, 108), 'mtn1': (134, 102, 124), 'mtn2': (238, 144, 116), 'snow0': (176, 152, 192),
    # far forest
    'for0': (34, 30, 42), 'for1': (84, 70, 64),
    # grass: lit (ochre) ramp and shade (olive/black) ramp share the bottom
    'k': (4, 4, 2), 'g0': (26, 32, 8), 'h1': (46, 46, 26), 'g1': (62, 58, 22), 'h2': (88, 84, 48),
    'g2': (116, 90, 36), 'g3': (160, 116, 58), 'g4': (198, 134, 72), 'g5': (232, 174, 102),
    'sedge': (122, 122, 60),
    # granite: violet shadow ramp, warm grey-peach light ramp
    'r0': (40, 34, 48), 'r1': (78, 68, 90), 'r2': (120, 104, 120), 'r3': (178, 146, 142), 'r4': (228, 188, 166),
    # water: reflected sky (cooled, darker), deep water / bed
    'w0': (52, 44, 58), 'w1': (160, 136, 170), 'w2': (184, 150, 174), 'w3': (206, 162, 164),
    'glint': (226, 202, 216),
}
NAMES = list(PAL.keys())
assert len(NAMES) == 32, len(NAMES)
IDX = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(NAMES)}
RGB = np.array([PAL[n] for n in NAMES], np.uint8)


def srgb2lab(c):
    c = np.asarray(c, float) / 255
    l = np.where(c <= 0.04045, c / 12.92, ((c + 0.055) / 1.055) ** 2.4)
    M = np.array([[0.4124, 0.3576, 0.1805], [0.2126, 0.7152, 0.0722], [0.0193, 0.1192, 0.9505]])
    xyz = l @ M.T / np.array([0.95047, 1.0, 1.08883])
    f = np.where(xyz > 0.008856, np.cbrt(xyz), 7.787 * xyz + 16 / 116)
    return np.stack([116 * f[..., 1] - 16, 500 * (f[..., 0] - f[..., 1]), 200 * (f[..., 1] - f[..., 2])], -1)


LAB = srgb2lab(RGB)
LV = LAB[:, 0]

RAMPS = {
    'sky': ['sky0', 'sky1', 'sky2', 'sky3', 'sky4', 'hot'],       # indexed by hue position, not value
    'mtn_shade': ['mtn0', 'mtn1'], 'mtn_lit': ['mtn1', 'mtn2'],
    'snow_shade': ['snow0', 'sky1'], 'snow_lit': ['sky3', 'sky4', 'hot'],
    'forest': ['for0', 'for1'],
    'grass_lit': ['g1', 'g2', 'g3', 'g4', 'g5'],
    'grass_shade': ['k', 'g0', 'h1', 'g1', 'h2'],
    'rock_shade': ['r0', 'r1', 'r2'], 'rock_lit': ['r2', 'r3', 'r4'],
}
RAMP_IDX = {k: np.array([IDX[n] for n in v]) for k, v in RAMPS.items()}

LIGHTS = {  # direction TO the sun (x right, y up, z away from camera); sun ~7 degrees up
    'left': (-0.93, 0.13, 0.30),
    'front': (0.35, 0.13, -0.93),
    'right': (0.93, 0.13, 0.30),
    'back': (-0.30, 0.13, 0.95),
}
SIZES = {'small': 0.55, 'medium': 0.95, 'large': 1.45}       # boulder radius (m): 1.1 / 1.9 / 2.9 m across
DISTANCES = {'close': 4.5, '10m': 10.0, '40m': 40.0}


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# noise & threshold maps (the cluster vocabulary)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Noise:
    def __init__(self, seed):
        self.rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.perm = self.rng.permutation(4096)
        self.grad = self.rng.normal(size=(4096, 3))
        self.grad /= np.linalg.norm(self.grad, axis=1, keepdims=True)

    def _h(self, ix, iy, iz):
        return self.perm[(ix * 73856093 ^ iy * 19349663 ^ iz * 83492791) & 4095]

    def value3(self, x, y, z):
        """smooth gradient noise in 3D, ~[-1,1]"""
        x, y, z = np.asarray(x, float), np.asarray(y, float), np.asarray(z, float)
        ix, iy, iz = np.floor(x).astype(np.int64), np.floor(y).astype(np.int64), np.floor(z).astype(np.int64)
        fx, fy, fz = x - ix, y - iy, z - iz
        u = fx * fx * (3 - 2 * fx); v = fy * fy * (3 - 2 * fy); w = fz * fz * (3 - 2 * fz)
        out = 0
        for dx in (0, 1):
            for dy in (0, 1):
                for dz in (0, 1):
                    g = self.grad[self._h(ix + dx, iy + dy, iz + dz)]
                    d = g[..., 0] * (fx - dx) + g[..., 1] * (fy - dy) + g[..., 2] * (fz - dz)
                    wt = (u if dx else 1 - u) * (v if dy else 1 - v) * (w if dz else 1 - w)
                    out = out + wt * d
        return out * 1.8

    def fbm3(self, x, y, z, oct=4):
        s, a, f = 0, 1.0, 1.0
        for _ in range(oct):
            s = s + a * self.value3(x * f, y * f, z * f); a *= 0.5; f *= 2.03
        return s

    def fbm2(self, x, y, oct=4, z=0.37):
        return self.fbm3(x, y, np.full(np.shape(x), z), oct)


def rank_uniform(a):
    """rank-normalise a map to a uniform [0,1) threshold map (so densities equal fractions)"""
    r = np.empty(a.size); r[np.argsort(a, axis=None)] = np.arange(a.size)
    return (r / a.size).reshape(a.shape)


def threshold_maps(noise, rng):
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W].astype(float)
    T = {}
    T['checker'] = ((xx + yy) % 2) * 0.5 + 0.25
    b4 = np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) / 16 + 1 / 32
    T['bayer'] = b4[yy.astype(int) % 4, xx.astype(int) % 4]
    T['speckle'] = rng.random((H, W))
    # vertical strokes (grass): noise stretched vertically
    T['vert'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2(xx / 0.8, yy / 3.5, 2, 1.1) + 0.35 * rng.random((H, W)))
    # horizontal ripples (water)
    T['horiz'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2(xx / 7.0, yy / 0.7, 2, 2.3) + 0.25 * rng.random((H, W)))
    # diagonal strokes (rock), two orientations
    T['diag_r'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2((xx + yy) / 3.5, (xx - yy) / 0.9, 2, 3.1) + 0.3 * rng.random((H, W)))
    T['diag_l'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2((xx - yy) / 3.5, (xx + yy) / 0.9, 2, 4.7) + 0.3 * rng.random((H, W)))
    return T


def ramp_pick(ramp, L, T, w, mask=None):
    """map target values L onto a ramp; in-between values get the pattern T (strength w in 0..1:
    0 = hard bands, 1 = full ordered/noise dither). Returns palette indices."""
    idx = RAMP_IDX[ramp]; lv = LV[idx]
    pos = np.interp(L, lv, np.arange(len(idx)))
    lo = np.floor(pos).astype(int); fr = pos - lo
    lo = np.clip(lo, 0, len(idx) - 1); hi = np.clip(lo + 1, 0, len(idx) - 1)
    up = fr > 0.5 + (T - 0.5) * w
    return np.where(up, idx[hi], idx[lo])


def pos_pick(ramp, pos, T, w):
    """like ramp_pick but with a ramp position given directly (used for the sky's hue sweep)"""
    idx = RAMP_IDX[ramp]
    lo = np.clip(np.floor(pos).astype(int), 0, len(idx) - 1); fr = pos - np.floor(pos)
    hi = np.clip(lo + 1, 0, len(idx) - 1)
    return np.where(fr > 0.5 + (T - 0.5) * w, idx[hi], idx[lo])


def smoothstep(a, b, x):
    t = np.clip((x - a) / (b - a), 0, 1); return t * t * (3 - 2 * t)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
# scene
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Scene:
    def __init__(self, size='medium', light='left', distance='close', seed=1):
        self.p = dict(size=size, light=light, distance=distance, seed=seed)
        self.rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.noise = Noise(seed)
        self.r = SIZES[size]
        self.l = np.array(LIGHTS[light], float); self.l /= np.linalg.norm(self.l)
        self.light = light
        d = DISTANCES[distance]
        self.bz = d
        # boulder sits left of centre (a third in), at the water's edge; its bank comes in from the left
        u_target = W * (0.40 + 0.04 * self.rng.uniform(-1, 1))
        self.bx = (u_target - W / 2) / F * d
        self.rx, self.ry, self.rz = self.r * 1.05, self.r * 0.80, self.r * 0.95
        self.ground_at_boulder = 0.06
        self.by = self.ground_at_boulder + 0.30 * self.ry          # ~35% sunk into the bank
        self.shore_seed = self.rng.uniform(0, 100)
        self.mtn_seed = self.rng.uniform(0, 100)

    # ------------------------------------------------ land: a bank coming in from the left
    def land_sd(self, x, z):
        """signed distance (m, >0 on land) of the boulder's bank; shoreline wraps the boulder's front"""
        n = self.noise
        r = self.r
        # the shoreline runs from the boulder's front-right corner back and away, and forward-left
        cx, cz = self.bx + 0.35 * r, self.bz - 0.15 * r
        ang = np.arctan2(z - cz, x - cx)
        # a line through (cx,cz): land is to the left of a gently curving line tilted away from camera
        d_line = -(x - cx) * 0.80 + (z - cz) * 0.60
        wobble = 0.35 * n.fbm2(x * 0.35 + self.shore_seed, z * 0.35, 3) * (1 + 0.04 * np.abs(z - cz))
        sd = d_line + wobble
        # the spit is broad behind the boulder and narrows toward the camera (water in front of it)
        sd = np.minimum(sd, (z - (self.bz - 1.2 * r - 0.6)) * 1.0 + 0.6 * np.maximum(0, -(x - cx)) * 0.25)
        return sd

    def land_h(self, x, z):
        sd = self.land_sd(x, z)
        n = self.noise
        h = 0.55 * smoothstep(0.0, 3.0 + self.r, sd) + 0.10 * n.fbm2(x * 0.5, z * 0.5 + 7, 3) * smoothstep(0.3, 1.5, sd)
        return np.where(sd > 0, h, -0.3 + 0.05 * sd)

    # ------------------------------------------------ boulder SDF (weathered, faceted)
    def boulder_sd(self, p):
        c = np.array([self.bx, self.by, self.bz])
        q = (p - c) / np.array([self.rx, self.ry, self.rz])
        k = np.linalg.norm(q, axis=-1)
        n = self.noise; s = 1.3 / self.r
        disp = 0.09 * n.fbm3(p[..., 0] * s, p[..., 1] * s, p[..., 2] * s, 3) \
            + 0.035 * n.value3(p[..., 0] * s * 3.1, p[..., 1] * s * 3.1, p[..., 2] * s * 3.1)
        # weathering flattens the top a little
        flat = np.maximum(0, q[..., 1] - 0.65) * 0.25
        return (k - 1 + flat) * self.r * 0.82 + disp * self.r

    def boulder_normal(self, p):
        e = 0.01 * self.r
        g = np.zeros_like(p)
        for i in range(3):
            dp = np.zeros(3); dp[i] = e
            g[..., i] = self.boulder_sd(p + dp) - self.boulder_sd(p - dp)
        return g / (np.linalg.norm(g, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9)

    def land_normal(self, x, z):
        e = 0.05
        hx = (self.land_h(x + e, z) - self.land_h(x - e, z)) / (2 * e)
        hz = (self.land_h(x, z + e) - self.land_h(x, z - e)) / (2 * e)
        nrm = np.stack([-hx, np.ones_like(hx), -hz], -1)
        return nrm / np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=-1, keepdims=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------ ray casting
    def camera_rays(self):
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W].astype(float)
        dx = xx + 0.5 - W / 2; dy = -(yy + 0.5 - H / 2)
        d = np.stack([dx, dy, np.full_like(dx, F)], -1)
        c, s = np.cos(-PITCH), np.sin(-PITCH)
        R = np.array([[1, 0, 0], [0, c, -s], [0, s, c]])
        d = d @ R.T
        return d / np.linalg.norm(d, axis=-1, keepdims=True)

    def march(self, o, d, tmax=260.0):
        """march rays; returns t, kind (0 none, 1 land, 2 boulder, 3 water)"""
        N = d.shape[0]
        t = np.full(N, 0.05); kind = np.zeros(N, int); alive = np.ones(N, bool)
        # water plane
        tw = np.where(d[:, 1] < -1e-6, -o[:, 1] / np.minimum(d[:, 1], -1e-6), np.inf)
        prev_t = t.copy()
        for it in range(420):
            idx = np.flatnonzero(alive)
            if idx.size == 0: break
            p = o[idx] + d[idx] * t[idx, None]
            sb = self.boulder_sd(p)
            hb = self.land_h(p[:, 0], p[:, 2])
            below = p[:, 1] < hb
            hitb = sb < 0.004 * max(1.0, t[idx].mean() * 0.02)
            # land hit (bisection refine later coarse is fine at these scales)
            hl = below & (hb > -0.001)
            hw = t[idx] >= tw[idx]
            done = hitb | hl | hw | (t[idx] > tmax)
            kind[idx[hitb]] = 2
            kl = idx[hl & ~hitb]; kind[kl] = 1
            kw = idx[hw & ~hitb & ~hl]; kind[kw] = 3; t[kw] = tw[kw]
            # refine land hits by bisection between prev_t and t
            if kl.size:
                a, b = prev_t[kl], t[kl]
                for _ in range(8):
                    m = (a + b) / 2; pm = o[kl] + d[kl] * m[:, None]
                    inside = pm[:, 1] < self.land_h(pm[:, 0], pm[:, 2])
                    b = np.where(inside, m, b); a = np.where(inside, a, m)
                t[kl] = b
            alive[idx[done]] = False
            rem = idx[~done]
            if rem.size == 0: break
            step = np.minimum(np.abs(sb[~done]) * 0.9 + 0.002, 0.02 + t[rem] * 0.012)
            prev_t[rem] = t[rem]
            t[rem] = t[rem] + np.maximum(step, 0.004)
        kind[(kind == 0) & np.isfinite(tw)] = 3
        t = np.where((kind == 3), np.minimum(t, tw), t)
        return t, kind

    def boulder_shadow(self, p):
        """soft visibility of the sun from points p (boulder occludes; low sun -> long shadows)"""
        vis = np.ones(p.shape[0]); t = np.full(p.shape[0], 0.03)
        for _ in range(80):
            q = p + self.l[None] * t[:, None]
            s = self.boulder_sd(q)
            vis = np.minimum(vis, np.clip(10 * s / t, 0, 1))
            t = t + np.clip(s, 0.02, 0.5 * self.r)
            if (t > 12 * self.r).all(): break
        return vis

    # ------------------------------------------------ backdrop (sky, mountain, forest) in (u, v)
    # u = column, v = rows above the horizon (screen pixel units), valid also for reflections.
    def forest_profile(self):
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] * 7 + 3)
        u = np.arange(W)
        top = np.full(W, 2.5 + rng.random() * 1.5)          # a low dark hedge
        top = top + 1.2 * self.noise.fbm2(u / 9.0, np.full(W, 3.3), 2)
        trees = []
        x = -4.0
        while x < W + 4:
            x += rng.uniform(2.0, 7.5)
            hgt = rng.uniform(6, 16) * (1.4 if rng.random() < 0.18 else 1.0)
            trees.append((x, hgt, rng.uniform(0.26, 0.34)))
        return top, trees

    def backdrop(self, u, v):
        """material / value / aux for backdrop points. returns dict of arrays."""
        n = self.noise
        u = np.asarray(u, float); v = np.asarray(v, float)
        mat = np.zeros(u.shape, int)      # 0 sky, 1 mountain, 2 forest
        # sky: position along the violet->peach ramp. brightest/warmest near the horizon on the sun side
        az_sun = np.arctan2(self.l[0], self.l[2])
        az = np.arctan2((u - W / 2) / F, 1.0)
        warm = np.cos(az - az_sun)                           # 1 toward sun, -1 away
        e = np.clip(v / 60.0, 0, 1.5)
        pos = 3.9 - 3.3 * e ** 0.8 + 0.9 * warm * np.exp(-e * 1.5)
        if self.light == 'front':                            # sun behind camera: belt of Venus
            pos = 2.6 - 2.4 * np.clip(e, 0, 1) + 1.0 * np.exp(-((v - 12) / 9.0) ** 2) - 1.2 * np.exp(-(v / 3.5) ** 2)
        if self.light == 'back':                             # sun just above the frame: glow
            pos = pos + 0.9 * np.exp(-e * 1.0) * np.clip(warm, 0, 1) ** 4
        sky_pos = np.clip(pos, 0, 5.0)
        # mountain ridge
        ms = self.mtn_seed
        uu = u / W
        peak_u = 0.72 if self.bx < 0 else 0.28
        ridge = 14 + 12 * np.exp(-((uu - peak_u) / 0.16) ** 2) + 5 * np.exp(-((uu - peak_u + 0.22) / 0.09) ** 2) \
            + 3.5 * n.fbm2(uu * 9 + ms, np.full(u.shape, 1.7), 4) + 1.2 * np.abs(n.fbm2(uu * 40 + ms, np.full(u.shape, 5.1), 2))
        in_m = v < ridge
        # mountain relief: height function; faces lit if they face the sun (screen-x component)
        hgt = (ridge - v) * 0.0 + n.fbm2(u / 7.0 + ms, v / 4.0, 4) * 3.0 - np.abs(n.fbm2(u / 16.0 + ms, v / 9.0 + 3, 3)) * 7
        gx = np.gradient(hgt, axis=-1) if hgt.ndim == 2 and hgt.shape[-1] > 1 else np.zeros_like(hgt)
        # slope direction of the ridge line itself (lit flanks of peaks)
        dr = np.gradient(ridge, axis=-1) if ridge.ndim >= 1 and ridge.shape[-1] > 1 else np.zeros_like(ridge)
        sun_x = self.l[0]
        facing = -np.sign(sun_x) * (dr * 0.7 + gx * 0.5) if abs(sun_x) > 0.2 else 0.25 + 0.3 * gx
        if self.light == 'back': facing = facing * 0.3 - 0.45
        if self.light == 'front': facing = 0.35 + 0.25 * np.abs(gx) - 0.2 * np.abs(dr)
        snow = (v > ridge - 9 + 3 * n.fbm2(u / 5.0 + 9, v / 3.0, 3)) & (np.abs(dr) < 1.6) & in_m
        m_lit = facing > 0.15
        # forest (far shore) silhouettes
        top, trees = self._forest
        uc = np.clip(np.round(u).astype(int), 0, W - 1)
        f_top = top[uc]
        in_f = v < f_top
        f_lit = np.zeros(u.shape, bool)
        for (tx, hgt_t, wr) in trees:
            dy = hgt_t - v                                    # distance below the tip
            ok = (dy > 0) & (v > -0.5)
            if not ok.any(): continue
            tier = (np.floor(dy / 1.7) % 2)
            halfw = dy * wr * (1 + 0.35 * (((dy / 1.7) % 1.0) > 0.45)) + 0.35
            dx = u - tx
            inside = ok & (np.abs(dx) < halfw)
            in_f = in_f | inside
            side = dx * -np.sign(sun_x) if abs(sun_x) > 0.2 else -np.abs(dx)
            lit = inside & (side > 0.25 * halfw) & (tier == 0)
            if self.light == 'back': lit = inside & (np.abs(dx) > halfw - 1.0) & (dy > 1)
            f_lit = f_lit | lit
        mat = np.where(in_m, 1, 0); mat = np.where(in_f, 2, mat)
        return dict(mat=mat, sky_pos=sky_pos, m_lit=m_lit, snow=snow, f_lit=f_lit, ridge=ridge, v=v)

    def backdrop_colour_L(self, bd):
        """continuous target Lab-ish value (for reflections): return palette indices of a flat reading"""
        mat = bd['mat']
        out = np.zeros(mat.shape, int)
        sp = np.clip(np.round(bd['sky_pos']).astype(int), 0, 5)
        out = RAMP_IDX['sky'][sp]
        mt = np.where(bd['snow'], np.where(bd['m_lit'], IDX['sky4'], IDX['snow0']),
                      np.where(bd['m_lit'], IDX['mtn2'], IDX['mtn0']))
        out = np.where(mat == 1, mt, out)
        out = np.where(mat == 2, np.where(bd['f_lit'], IDX['for1'], IDX['for0']), out)
        return out

    # ------------------------------------------------ render
    def render(self):
        T = threshold_maps(self.noise, self.rng)
        self._forest = self.forest_profile()
        d = self.camera_rays().reshape(-1, 3)
        o = np.tile([0.0, HC, 0.0], (d.shape[0], 1))
        t, kind = self.march(o, d)
        p = o + d * t[:, None]
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
        img = np.zeros(H * W, int)
        Lt = np.zeros(H * W)             # target value (for analysis / debugging)
        self.gb = dict(kind=kind.reshape(H, W), t=t.reshape(H, W))
        vrow = (Y_H - (yy + 0.5)).ravel()

        # ---------------- backdrop: sky, mountain, far forest
        sky = kind == 0
        bd = self.backdrop(xx.ravel()[sky].astype(float), vrow[sky])
        img[sky] = self.paint_backdrop(bd, T, yy.ravel()[sky], xx.ravel()[sky])

        # ---------------- land (bank) & boulder
        self.paint_land(img, Lt, kind, p, d, T)
        self.paint_boulder(img, Lt, kind, p, d, T)

        # ---------------- water: reflection
        self.paint_water(img, kind, p, d, T, xx.ravel(), yy.ravel())

        img = img.reshape(H, W)
        # ---------------- strokes: grass blades & sedge, back to front
        img = self.paint_strokes(img, kind.reshape(H, W), p.reshape(H, W, 3), t.reshape(H, W))
        # ---------------- near repoussoir bank (dark, bottom corner opposite the boulder)
        img = self.paint_repoussoir(img)
        self.index = img
        return RGB[img]

    # ------------------------------------------------ painters
    def paint_backdrop(self, bd, T, yy, xx, reflect=False):
        mat = bd['mat']
        Tc = T['checker'][yy, xx]
        out = pos_pick('sky', bd['sky_pos'], Tc, 0.32)             # flat bands, checker seams
        # mountain: lit faces warm, shadow faces violet; transitions in checker, strokes diagonal
        Td = (T['diag_r'] if self.l[0] < 0 else T['diag_l'])[yy, xx]
        m_rock_L = np.where(bd['m_lit'], 60.0, 40.0) + 6 * (bd['ridge'] - bd['v'] < 1.5) * bd['m_lit']
        rock = np.where(bd['m_lit'], ramp_pick('mtn_lit', m_rock_L, Td, 0.9), ramp_pick('mtn_shade', m_rock_L, Td, 0.9))
        snowc = np.where(bd['m_lit'], ramp_pick('snow_lit', np.full(mat.shape, 76.0), Tc, 0.6),
                         ramp_pick('snow_shade', np.full(mat.shape, 66.5), Tc, 0.5))
        mcol = np.where(bd['snow'], snowc, rock)
        # sun-side crest line: 1px bright line along the top edge of lit slopes
        crest = (bd['ridge'] - bd['v'] < 1.0) & bd['m_lit']
        mcol = np.where(crest, IDX['mtn2'] if not self.light == 'back' else IDX['hot'], mcol)
        out = np.where(mat == 1, mcol, out)
        fcol = np.where(bd['f_lit'], IDX['for1'], IDX['for0'])
        out = np.where(mat == 2, fcol, out)
        return out

    def paint_land(self, img, Lt, kind, p, d, T):
        m = kind == 1
        if not m.any(): return
        pl = p[m]
        nrm = self.land_normal(pl[:, 0], pl[:, 2])
        ndl = np.clip((nrm * self.l).sum(-1), 0, 1)
        vis = self.boulder_shadow(pl + nrm * 0.02)
        direct = ndl * vis
        # grass at a grazing sun: light depends little on slope, strongly on shadow; back light glows
        viewh = -d[m]; viewh[:, 1] = 0; viewh /= np.linalg.norm(viewh, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        back = np.clip(-(viewh * self.l).sum(-1), 0, 1)       # 1 when looking into the sun
        z = np.linalg.norm(pl - np.array([0, HC, 0]), axis=1)
        lit_amt = smoothstep(0.02, 0.12, direct)
        # occlusion around the boulder foot (contact shadow)
        c = np.array([self.bx, self.by, self.bz])
        dist_b = self.boulder_sd(pl)
        occl = smoothstep(0.0, 0.45 * self.r, dist_b)
        L_lit = 46 + 12 * np.sqrt(ndl) + 8 * back + 5 * smoothstep(8, 40, z)
        L_sh = 12 + 10 * smoothstep(3, 40, z) + 5 * back
        L_sh = L_sh * (0.35 + 0.65 * occl)
        L_lit = L_lit * (0.6 + 0.4 * occl)
        yy, xx = np.divmod(np.flatnonzero(m), W)
        Tv = T['vert'][yy, xx]
        # far grass reads as speckle, near grass as vertical strokes
        near = z < 18
        Tpat = np.where(near, Tv, T['speckle'][yy, xx])
        lit_i = ramp_pick('grass_lit', L_lit, Tpat, 1.0)
        sh_i = ramp_pick('grass_shade', L_sh, Tpat, 1.0)
        # terminator between lit and shade is made of strokes too
        use_lit = lit_amt > Tpat * 0.9 + 0.05
        img[m] = np.where(use_lit, lit_i, sh_i)
        Lt[m] = np.where(use_lit, L_lit, L_sh)
        self.land_lit = np.zeros(H * W); self.land_lit[m] = lit_amt
        self.land_back = back.mean() if back.size else 0

    def paint_boulder(self, img, Lt, kind, p, d, T):
        m = kind == 2
        self.boulder_mask = m.reshape(H, W)
        if not m.any(): return
        pb = p[m]
        n_s = self.boulder_normal(pb)
        # facets: snap normals toward the mean normal of Voronoi cells on the surface ("planes turned in space")
        cell = 0.36 * self.r
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] + 11)
        q = pb / cell
        base = np.floor(q).astype(int)
        best = np.full(len(pb), np.inf); cid = np.zeros(len(pb), np.int64)
        for dx in (-1, 0, 1):
            for dy in (-1, 0, 1):
                for dz in (-1, 0, 1):
                    cc = base + np.array([dx, dy, dz])
                    h = (cc[:, 0] * 73856093 ^ cc[:, 1] * 19349663 ^ cc[:, 2] * 83492791) & 0xFFFFFF
                    jit = np.stack([(h % 97) / 97, ((h // 97) % 89) / 89, ((h // 8633) % 83) / 83], 1)
                    dd = np.linalg.norm(q - (cc + jit), axis=1)
                    better = dd < best
                    best = np.where(better, dd, best); cid = np.where(better, h, cid)
        uniq, inv = np.unique(cid, return_inverse=True)
        mean_n = np.zeros((len(uniq), 3)); np.add.at(mean_n, inv, n_s)
        mean_n /= np.linalg.norm(mean_n, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        n_f = 0.65 * mean_n[inv] + 0.35 * n_s
        n_f /= np.linalg.norm(n_f, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        ndl = (n_f * self.l).sum(-1)
        vis = self.boulder_shadow(pb + n_s * 0.03)
        direct = np.clip(ndl, 0, 1) * vis
        sky_amb = 0.5 + 0.5 * n_f[:, 1]
        bounce = np.clip(-n_f[:, 1], 0, 1)
        hg = pb[:, 1] - self.land_h(pb[:, 0], pb[:, 2]).clip(0, None)
        occl = smoothstep(0.0, 0.35 * self.r, hg)
        L_lit = 52 + 32 * direct ** 0.7
        core = np.exp(-((ndl + 0.05) / 0.2) ** 2)
        L_sh = (16 + 12 * sky_amb + 7 * bounce - 9 * core) * (0.45 + 0.55 * occl)
        lit = direct > 0.06
        yy, xx = np.divmod(np.flatnonzero(m), W)
        # stroke direction on rock follows the facet's slope in screen space
        Td = np.where(n_f[:, 0] * n_f[:, 1] > 0, T['diag_l'][yy, xx], T['diag_r'][yy, xx])
        Tc = T['checker'][yy, xx]
        Tm = np.where(self.r * F / self.bz > 25, Td, Tc)
        li = ramp_pick('rock_lit', L_lit, Tm, 0.85)
        si = ramp_pick('rock_shade', L_sh, Tm, 0.85)
        col = np.where(lit, li, si)
        # facet crest lines: where the facet id changes and this side is the lit one -> r4 line; creases -> r0
        cid_img = np.full(H * W, -1, np.int64); cid_img[m] = cid
        ndl_img = np.full(H * W, -9.0); ndl_img[m] = ndl
        cimg = cid_img.reshape(H, W); nimg = ndl_img.reshape(H, W)
        edge = np.zeros((H, W), bool); brighter = np.zeros((H, W), bool)
        for sy, sx in ((0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)):
            c2 = np.roll(np.roll(cimg, sy, 0), sx, 1); n2 = np.roll(np.roll(nimg, sy, 0), sx, 1)
            e = (c2 != cimg) & (c2 >= 0) & (cimg >= 0)
            edge |= e; brighter |= e & (nimg > n2 + 0.12)
        ed = edge.ravel()[m]; br = brighter.ravel()[m]
        big = self.r * F / self.bz > 14                       # detail only when the boulder is big enough
        rr = self.rng.random(len(col))
        if big:
            col = np.where(br & lit & (rr < 0.75), IDX['r4'], col)
            col = np.where(ed & ~br & ~lit & (rr < 0.45), IDX['r0'], col)
            # granite speckle: dark specks in light, light specks in shade
            col = np.where(lit & (rr > 0.965), IDX['r2'], col)
            col = np.where(~lit & (rr > 0.985) & (L_sh > 22), IDX['r2'], col)
        # sunlit rim on the silhouette when back-lit
        if self.light == 'back':
            sil = self.boulder_mask & ~(np.roll(self.boulder_mask, 1, 0) & np.roll(self.boulder_mask, 1, 1) & np.roll(self.boulder_mask, -1, 1))
            s = sil.ravel()[m] & (n_f[:, 1] > -0.1)
            col = np.where(s, IDX['r4'], col)
        img[m] = col
        Lt[m] = np.where(lit, L_lit, L_sh)
        self.b_lit = np.zeros(H * W, bool); self.b_lit[m] = lit

    def paint_water(self, img, kind, p, d, T, xx, yy):
        m = kind == 3
        if not m.any(): return
        pw = p[m]; dw = d[m].copy()
        dr = dw * np.array([1, -1, 1])
        o = pw + dr * 0.01
        t2, k2 = self.march(o, dr, tmax=200)
        p2 = o + dr * t2[:, None]
        src = np.zeros(len(pw), int)
        # reflected backdrop: the column stays, the elevation mirrors
        e = np.arcsin(np.clip(dr[:, 1], -1, 1))
        v_up = F * (np.tan(e + PITCH) - np.tan(PITCH))
        sky = k2 == 0
        if sky.any():
            bd = self.backdrop(xx[m][sky].astype(float), v_up[sky])
            src[sky] = self.backdrop_colour_L(bd)
            self._refl_skypos = bd['sky_pos']
        # reflected boulder & land: re-shade with the same light, flat reading
        for kk in (1, 2):
            s = k2 == kk
            if not s.any(): continue
            pp = p2[s]
            if kk == 1:
                nrm = self.land_normal(pp[:, 0], pp[:, 2]); ndl = np.clip((nrm * self.l).sum(-1), 0, 1)
                vis = self.boulder_shadow(pp + nrm * 0.02); lit = ndl * vis > 0.05
                src[s] = np.where(lit, IDX['g3'], IDX['g0'])
            else:
                nrm = self.boulder_normal(pp); ndl = (nrm * self.l).sum(-1)
                vis = self.boulder_shadow(pp + nrm * 0.03)
                lit = np.clip(ndl, 0, 1) * vis > 0.06
                hg = pp[:, 1] - self.ground_at_boulder
                src[s] = np.where(lit, np.where(ndl > 0.45, IDX['r4'], IDX['r3']), np.where(hg < 0.25 * self.r, IDX['r0'], IDX['r1']))
        # reflection transform measured on the reference: lights -13 L, mids -4.5, darks ~+1; cooler (a* -5)
        lab = LAB[src].copy()
        L = lab[:, 0]
        lab[:, 0] = L - 0.30 * np.maximum(0, L - 25) + 1.0 * (L < 25)
        lab[:, 1] -= 4.0
        # Fresnel: nearer water (steeper look) shows more of the dark water body
        dep = np.arcsin(np.clip(-dw[:, 1], 0, 1))
        kref = np.clip(1.0 - 1.9 * dep, 0.5, 0.97)
        lab = lab * kref[:, None] + LAB[IDX['w0']][None] * (1 - kref[:, None])
        # quantise onto the palette with horizontal ripple dither between the two nearest colours
        allowed = np.array([IDX[n] for n in ['k', 'for0', 'w0', 'r0', 'r1', 'mtn0', 'mtn1', 'r2', 'h1', 'g0', 'g1', 'g2', 'g3',
                                             'w1', 'w2', 'w3', 'sky1', 'sky2', 'r3', 'mtn2', 'snow0']])
        dd = np.linalg.norm(lab[:, None, :] - LAB[allowed][None], axis=-1)
        o2 = np.argsort(dd, axis=1)
        c1, c2 = allowed[o2[:, 0]], allowed[o2[:, 1]]
        d1, d2 = dd[np.arange(len(dd)), o2[:, 0]], dd[np.arange(len(dd)), o2[:, 1]]
        frac = d1 / (d1 + d2 + 1e-9)                           # 0 = exactly c1, 0.5 = halfway
        Th = T['horiz'][yy[m], xx[m]]; Tc = T['checker'][yy[m], xx[m]]
        Tr = np.where(LAB[c1][:, 0] > 55, Tc, Th)              # light water: checker, dark: horizontal
        col = np.where(frac > 0.5 + (Tr - 0.5) * 0.9, c2, c1)
        # streak lines: sparse horizontal 1px lines, denser toward the far shore
        r = self.rng.random(len(col))
        rowseed = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] + 5).random(H)
        vrow = yy[m]
        streak_row = rowseed[vrow] < 0.28 * np.exp(-(vrow - Y_H) / 60)
        seg = (np.sin(xx[m] * 0.21 + rowseed[vrow] * 40) + 0.6 * np.sin(xx[m] * 0.07 + rowseed[vrow] * 11)) > 0.9
        light_src = LAB[col][:, 0] > 50
        col = np.where(streak_row & seg & ~light_src, IDX['w1'], col)
        col = np.where(streak_row & seg & light_src & (r < 0.8), IDX['w0'] if False else IDX['glint'], col)
        img[m] = col

    def paint_strokes(self, img, kind, p, t):
        """grass blades & sedge, back to front, in counterchange with what lies behind"""
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] + 101)
        out = img.copy()
        land = kind == 1
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(land)
        if len(ys) == 0: return out
        z = t[ys, xs]
        pl = p[ys, xs]
        # blade world height: short turf; sedge near the boulder foot and at the waterline
        db = self.boulder_sd(pl)
        sd = self.land_sd(pl[:, 0], pl[:, 2])
        near_b = np.exp(-np.maximum(db, 0) / (0.45 * self.r))
        near_w = np.exp(-np.maximum(sd, 0) / 0.5)
        hb = 0.07 + 0.45 * np.maximum(near_b, 0.7 * near_w) * self.r ** 0.3
        Lpx = F * hb / z
        dens = np.clip(0.10 + 0.5 * np.maximum(near_b, near_w), 0, 0.8) * (Lpx >= 1.5)
        spawn = rng.random(len(ys)) < dens
        order = np.argsort(ys[spawn], kind='stable')           # back (top) to front (bottom)
        bys, bxs, bL = ys[spawn][order], xs[spawn][order], Lpx[spawn][order]
        lit_here = self.land_lit.reshape(H, W)[bys, bxs] > 0.5
        lean_field = self.noise.fbm2(bxs / 23.0, bys / 17.0, 2, 9.1) * 0.55
        LVimg = LV
        sun_side = -np.sign(self.l[0]) if abs(self.l[0]) > 0.2 else 0
        for i in range(len(bys)):
            y0, x0 = bys[i], bxs[i]
            L = int(np.clip(round(bL[i] * rng.uniform(0.55, 1.25)), 1, 26))
            ang = lean_field[i] + rng.normal(0, 0.18)
            curl = rng.normal(0, 0.035)
            x = float(x0); y = y0
            bg = out[y0, x0]
            blit = lit_here[i]
            for k in range(L):
                yy = y - k
                if yy < 0: break
                xi = int(round(x))
                if not (0 <= xi < W): break
                behind = LVimg[out[yy, xi]]
                tip = k >= L - max(1, L // 4)
                # counterchange: dark blade over light, light blade over dark
                if behind > 42:
                    c = IDX['g2'] if (blit and rng.random() < 0.55) else IDX['g0']
                    if kind[yy, xi] == 2 or kind[yy, xi] == 0 or kind[yy, xi] == 3:
                        c = IDX['g0'] if behind > 55 else IDX['h1']
                else:
                    if blit:
                        c = IDX['g4'] if tip else IDX['g2']
                        if self.light == 'back' and tip: c = IDX['g5']
                    else:
                        c = IDX['h2'] if tip else (IDX['g1'] if behind > 16 else IDX['h1'])
                        if kind[yy, xi] != 1: c = IDX['sedge'] if tip else IDX['g1']
                out[yy, xi] = c
                ang += curl
                x += np.tan(np.clip(ang, -1.1, 1.1))
            # reflection of blades rooted at the waterline
            if near_w[spawn][order][i] > 0.6 and L >= 3:
                x = float(x0)
                for k in range(1, L):
                    yy = y0 + k
                    if yy >= H: break
                    xi = int(round(x + (x - x0) * 0))
                    if 0 <= xi < W and kind[yy, xi] == 3:
                        out[yy, xi] = IDX['for0'] if LVimg[out[yy, xi]] > 30 else IDX['g1']
        return out

    def paint_repoussoir(self, img):
        """dark near bank in the corner opposite the boulder, grass silhouettes against the water"""
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] + 202)
        out = img.copy()
        right = self.bx < 0
        xs = np.arange(W)
        xr = (xs if right else (W - 1 - xs)) / W        # 0 at the far side .. 1 at the corner
        edge = H - (np.clip(xr - 0.62, 0, None) * 150) - 4 * self.noise.fbm2(xs / 13.0, np.full(W, 8.8), 2)
        edge = edge.astype(float)
        yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W]
        m = yy >= edge[None, :]
        # base: black & dark olive in vertical strokes (shade bank, like Ferrari's right bank)
        T = rank_uniform(self.noise.fbm2(xx / 0.8, yy / 3.2, 2, 5.5) + 0.4 * rng.random((H, W)))
        depth = (yy - edge[None, :]) / 20.0
        L = 20 - 12 * np.clip(depth, 0, 1) + 6 * (T > 0.8)
        base = ramp_pick('grass_shade', L, T, 1.0)
        out[m] = base[m]
        # blades rising out of the top edge, silhouetted against water
        lit_side = (self.l[0] > 0.2) == right or self.light == 'front'
        for x0 in range(W):
            if not (edge[x0] < H): continue
            nb = rng.poisson(0.9)
            for _ in range(nb):
                y0 = int(edge[x0] + rng.uniform(0, 6))
                L = int(rng.uniform(4, 16))
                ang = rng.normal(0.25 if right else -0.25, 0.3); curl = rng.normal(0, 0.05)
                x = float(x0)
                for k in range(L):
                    y = y0 - k; xi = int(round(x))
                    if y < 0 or not (0 <= xi < W): break
                    tip = k > L * 0.7
                    out[y, xi] = (IDX['g3'] if (lit_side and tip) else IDX['g1']) if rng.random() < 0.9 else IDX['h2']
                    ang += curl; x += np.tan(np.clip(ang, -1, 1))
        return out


def paint(size='medium', light='left', distance='close', seed=1, return_scene=False):
    s = Scene(size, light, distance, seed)
    rgb = s.render()
    return (rgb, s) if return_scene else rgb


if __name__ == '__main__':
    import sys
    rgb = paint()
    Image.fromarray(np.repeat(np.repeat(rgb, 3, 0), 3, 1)).save(sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else 'test.png')

Bash: Rewrite grass-bank painter with texture spread and big light masses
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && python3 - <<'EOF'
f='src/paint/ferrari_method.py'
s=open(f).read()
s=s.replace("'grass_lit': ['g1', 'g2', 'g3', 'g4', 'g5'],","'grass_lit': ['g0', 'g1', 'g2', 'g3', 'g4', 'g5'],")

s=s[:a]+'''    def paint_land(self, img, Lt, kind, p, d, T):
        m = kind == 1
        self.land_lit = np.zeros(H * W)
        if not m.any(): return
        pl = p[m]
        # light is judged on the LARGE form (smoothed normal): light and shade in big connected masses
        e = 0.45
        hx = (self.land_h(pl[:, 0] + e, pl[:, 2]) - self.land_h(pl[:, 0] - e, pl[:, 2])) / (2 * e)
        hz = (self.land_h(pl[:, 0], pl[:, 2] + e) - self.land_h(pl[:, 0], pl[:, 2] - e)) / (2 * e)
        nrm = np.stack([-hx, np.ones_like(hx), -hz], -1); nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        ndl = (nrm * self.l).sum(-1)
        vis = self.boulder_shadow(pl + np.array([0, 0.03, 0]))
        lit_amt = smoothstep(-0.06, 0.08, ndl) * smoothstep(0.35, 0.8, vis)
        viewh = -d[m].copy(); viewh[:, 1] = 0; viewh /= np.linalg.norm(viewh, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        back = np.clip(-(viewh * self.l).sum(-1), 0, 1)       # 1 when looking into the sun
        z = np.linalg.norm(pl - np.array([0, HC, 0]), axis=1)
        occl = smoothstep(0.0, 0.5 * self.r, self.boulder_sd(pl))
        far = smoothstep(6, 45, z)
        # value plan: lit grass mid-light, brightening as the plane recedes; shade grass near-black
        L_lit = (43 + 9 * np.sqrt(np.clip(ndl, 0, 1)) + 7 * back + 7 * far) * (0.55 + 0.45 * occl)
        L_sh = (10 + 9 * far + 4 * back) * (0.3 + 0.7 * occl)
        yy, xx = np.divmod(np.flatnonzero(m), W)
        near = smoothstep(30, 12, z)
        Tv = T['vert'][yy, xx]; Ts = T['speckle'][yy, xx]
        Ttex = np.where(near > 0.5, Tv, Ts)
        # texture spread: near grass spans base / mid stroke / dark gap (Ferrari: 60/40/24/10),
        # the spread shrinks with distance until grass is a two-colour speckle
        amp = 5 + 17 * near
        Lli = L_lit + amp * (Ttex * 2 - 1) * np.where(Ttex < 0.12, 1.6, 1.0)
        Lsh = L_sh + (0.6 * amp) * (Ttex * 2 - 1)
        Tq = T['checker'][yy, xx]
        lit_i = ramp_pick('grass_lit', Lli, Tq, 0.3)
        sh_i = ramp_pick('grass_shade', Lsh, Tq, 0.3)
        use_lit = lit_amt > T['vert'][yy, xx] * 0.8 + 0.1       # terminator made of strokes too
        img[m] = np.where(use_lit, lit_i, sh_i)
        Lt[m] = np.where(use_lit, L_lit, L_sh)
        self.land_lit[m] = lit_amt

'''+s[b:]
open(f,'w').write(s)
EOF
echo ok
