Bash: Create sheet generator and render light & distance sheets
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && cat > src/paint/sheets.py <<'EOF'
"""Render parameter sweep sheets. Cells are 320x180 native, shown 3x nearest-neighbour."""
import sys, os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
from multiprocessing import Pool
import ferrari_method as fm
ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))))
OUT = os.path.join(ROOT, 'sheets'); CELLS = os.path.join(OUT, 'cells')
os.makedirs(CELLS, exist_ok=True)
BASE = dict(size='medium', light='left', distance='10m', seed=1)

def key(p): return f"{p['size']}_{p['light']}_{p['distance']}_s{p['seed']}"

def render(p):
    fn = os.path.join(CELLS, key(p) + '.png')
    if not os.path.exists(fn) or os.environ.get('FORCE'):
        rgb, s = fm.paint(**p, return_scene=True)
        Image.fromarray(rgb).save(fn)
        np.save(fn.replace('.png', '_idx.npy'), s.index)
    return fn

def sheet(name, cells, cols, scale=3, title=''):
    fnt = ImageFont.truetype('/System/Library/Fonts/Menlo.ttc', 22)
    tiles = []
    for p, lab in cells:
        im = Image.open(os.path.join(CELLS, key(p) + '.png'))
        im = im.resize((fm.W * scale, fm.H * scale), Image.NEAREST)
        canvas = Image.new('RGB', (im.width, im.height + 34), (250, 248, 244))
        canvas.paste(im, (0, 34)); ImageDraw.Draw(canvas).text((6, 5), lab, fill=(20, 20, 20), font=fnt)
        tiles.append(canvas)
    rows = (len(tiles) + cols - 1) // cols; tw, th = tiles[0].size; g = 12
    S = Image.new('RGB', (cols * tw + (cols + 1) * g, rows * th + (rows + 1) * g + 46), (250, 248, 244))
    ImageDraw.Draw(S).text((g, 10), title, fill=(0, 0, 0), font=fnt)
    for i, t in enumerate(tiles):
        S.paste(t, (g + (i % cols) * (tw + g), 46 + g + (i // cols) * (th + g)))
    S.save(os.path.join(OUT, name))

def plan():
    sw = {}
    sw['sheet_size.png'] = ([dict(BASE, size=v) for v in ('small', 'medium', 'large')], 'size', 3)
    sw['sheet_light.png'] = ([dict(BASE, light=v) for v in ('left', 'front', 'right', 'back')], 'light', 2)
    sw['sheet_distance.png'] = ([dict(BASE, distance=v) for v in ('close', '10m', '40m')], 'distance', 3)
    sw['sheet_seed.png'] = ([dict(BASE, seed=v) for v in (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6)], 'seed', 3)
    sw['sheet_light_x_distance.png'] = ([dict(BASE, light=l, distance=d) for d in ('close', '10m', '40m') for l in ('left', 'front', 'right', 'back')], None, 4)
    return sw

if __name__ == '__main__':
    only = sys.argv[1:]
    sw = plan()
    if only: sw = {k: v for k, v in sw.items() if any(o in k for o in only)}
    todo = []
    for k, (ps, _, _) in sw.items():
        for p in ps:
            if p not in todo: todo.append(p)
    with Pool(4) as pool:
        pool.map(render, todo)
    for k, (ps, var, cols) in sw.items():
        cells = [(p, f"{var} = {p[var]}" if var else f"{p['light']} / {p['distance']}") for p in ps]
        sheet(k, cells, cols, title=f"{k[:-4]}   (base: medium, left, 10 m, seed 1; 320x180 native, 32 colours, 3x)")
    print('done', len(todo), 'cells')
EOF

"""Pixel-geometry of contours: how silhouettes resolve into runs (flat, 45deg, 2:1 ...)."""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from collections import Counter
def profile_runs(top):
    """top: y per column (int). Returns list of (run_len_x, step_y) segments: a horizontal run then a step."""
    segs=[]; run=1
    for i in range(1,len(top)):
        dy=int(top[i]-top[i-1])
        if dy==0: run+=1
        else: segs.append((run,dy)); run=1
    segs.append((run,0)); return segs
def classify(segs):
    c=Counter()
    for run,dy in segs:
        a=abs(dy)
        if dy==0: continue
        if run==1 and a==1: c['45deg (1:1)']+=1
        elif run==2 and a==1: c['2:1 (shallow)']+=1
        elif run==1 and a==2: c['1:2 (steep)']+=1
        elif run>=3 and a==1: c['long flat run then 1 step']+=1
        elif run==1 and a>=3: c['vertical drop >=3']+=1
        else: c['other']+=1
    tot=sum(c.values()); return {k:round(v/tot,3) for k,v in c.most_common()},tot
def top_profile(mask,x0,x1):
    out=[]
    for x in range(x0,x1):
        ys=np.flatnonzero(mask[:,x]); out.append(ys[0] if len(ys) else -1)
    return np.array(out)
if __name__=='__main__':
    a=load_ref(); P=np.load('out/planes.npy')
    nonsky=P!=0
    for name,(x0,x1) in {'mountain crest':(205,300),'mountain right shoulder':(318,405),'tree line (left group)':(0,160)}.items():
        t=top_profile(nonsky,x0,x1); segs=profile_runs(t); cl,n=classify(segs)
        print(name,n,cl)
        print('   runs(x) hist', Counter([r for r,d in segs]).most_common(8))
    # bottoms: island meeting water; find bottom boundary of island colour mass
    lab=rgb2lab(a)

Write: pedagogy/analysis/src/paint/pixelgeo.py
"""Pixel geometry: contours resolved into short straight runs with clean steps.

Measured on the reference (src/contours.py): Ferrari's mountain crest is 41% 1:1 steps and 53% flat
runs of 3-6 px then a single step; the right shoulder is 43% 1:1 and 29% 2:1.  Silhouettes are
straight segments with CONSISTENT step ratios, not noise.  These helpers impose the same grammar
on procedurally generated shapes: simplify the contour to a polygon, snap every edge to one of the
clean pixel slopes, solve for vertex positions that keep the shape, and re-rasterise.
"""
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from skimage import measure
from scipy.ndimage import distance_transform_edt

# clean slopes as (dx, dy) directions: flat, 3:1, 2:1, 1:1, 1:2, 1:3, vertical (both signs)
_BASE = [(1, 0), (3, 1), (2, 1), (1, 1), (1, 2), (1, 3), (0, 1)]
DIRS = []
for dx, dy in _BASE:
    for sx, sy in ((1, 1), (1, -1), (-1, 1), (-1, -1)):
        v = (dx * sx, dy * sy)
        if v not in DIRS: DIRS.append(v)
DIRS = np.array(DIRS, float)
UDIRS = DIRS / np.linalg.norm(DIRS, axis=1, keepdims=True)


def snap_polygon(P, prefer_flat=0.0):
    """P: (n,2) closed polygon (x,y). Returns a polygon whose edges all have clean slopes."""
    n = len(P)
    E = np.roll(P, -1, 0) - P
    L = np.linalg.norm(E, axis=1) + 1e-9
    U = E / L[:, None]
    cos = U @ UDIRS.T
    # a little bias toward flat runs (ground contacts, crests read level)
    cos = cos + prefer_flat * (np.abs(UDIRS[:, 1]) < 1e-6)[None]
    D = UDIRS[np.argmax(cos, 1)]
    # unknowns: v0 (2) + t_i (n); vertex i = v0 + sum_{j<i} t_j D_j
    A = np.zeros((2 * n + 2, 2 + n)); b = np.zeros(2 * n + 2)
    for i in range(n):
        A[2 * i, 0] = 1; A[2 * i + 1, 1] = 1
        for j in range(i):
            A[2 * i, 2 + j] = D[j, 0]; A[2 * i + 1, 2 + j] = D[j, 1]
        b[2 * i] = P[i, 0]; b[2 * i + 1] = P[i, 1]
    w = 30.0                                             # closure
    A[2 * n, 2:] = w * D[:, 0]; A[2 * n + 1, 2:] = w * D[:, 1]
    sol, *_ = np.linalg.lstsq(A, b, rcond=None)
    v0, t = sol[:2], sol[2:]
    V = [v0]
    for j in range(n - 1):
        V.append(V[-1] + t[j] * D[j])
    return np.array(V)


def clean_mask(mask, tol=1.1, min_area=6, prefer_flat=0.0):
    """re-rasterise a binary mask with clean-slope straight edges"""
    H, W = mask.shape
    pad = np.pad(mask.astype(float), 1)
    out = Image.new('L', (W, H), 0); d = ImageDraw.Draw(out)
    holes = []
    for c in measure.find_contours(pad, 0.5):
        c = c[:, ::-1] - 1                                # (x, y)
        if len(c) < 8: continue
        area = 0.5 * np.abs(np.dot(c[:, 0], np.roll(c[:, 1], 1)) - np.dot(c[:, 1], np.roll(c[:, 0], 1)))
        if area < min_area: continue
        poly = measure.approximate_polygon(c, tolerance=tol)[:-1]
        if len(poly) < 3: continue
        sp = snap_polygon(poly, prefer_flat)
        # orientation decides fill or hole: sample the original mask at the contour's interior side
        inside = measure.points_in_poly(np.argwhere(mask)[:, ::-1] + 0.0, c).mean() if False else None
        xy = [tuple(p) for p in np.round(sp + 0.0).astype(int)]
        cy, cx = np.round(c.mean(0)[::-1]).astype(int)
        signed = np.sum((np.roll(c[:, 0], -1) - c[:, 0]) * (np.roll(c[:, 1], -1) + c[:, 1]))
        (holes if signed > 0 else [None]).append(xy) if False else None
        rr = measure.grid_points_in_poly((H, W), c[:, ::-1])
        frac = mask[rr].mean() if rr.any() else 1
        if frac > 0.5: d.polygon(xy, fill=1)
        else: holes.append(xy)
    for xy in holes: d.polygon(xy, fill=0)
    return np.array(out) > 0


def refill(img, old, new, fallback=None):
    """pixels gained take the nearest old-inside colour; pixels lost take the nearest outside colour"""
    out = img.copy()
    gain = new & ~old; lose = old & ~new
    if gain.any():
        _, (iy, ix) = distance_transform_edt(~old, return_indices=True)
        out[gain] = img[iy[gain], ix[gain]]
    if lose.any():
        src = fallback if fallback is not None else img
        _, (iy, ix) = distance_transform_edt(old, return_indices=True)
        out[lose] = src[iy[lose], ix[lose]]
    return out


def polyline_profile(f, u0, u1, knots, rng, jitter=0.35):
    """sample f at knots, then join them with clean-slope segments (for ridges / crests).
    Returns y per integer column u in [u0, u1)."""
    us = np.linspace(u0, u1, knots)
    us[1:-1] += rng.uniform(-jitter, jitter, knots - 2) * (us[1] - us[0])
    ys = f(us)
    P = [(us[0], ys[0])]
    for i in range(1, len(us)):
        dx, dy = us[i] - P[-1][0], ys[i] - P[-1][1]
        # snap slope to the clean set (x must advance)
        cand = [(1, 0), (3, 1), (2, 1), (1, 1), (1, 2), (1, 3), (1, 5)]
        best = min(cand, key=lambda c: abs(np.arctan2(abs(dy), dx) - np.arctan2(c[1], c[0])))
        slope = np.sign(dy) * best[1] / best[0]
        P.append((us[i], P[-1][1] + slope * dx))
    P = np.array(P)
    u = np.arange(int(u0), int(u1))
    y = np.interp(u, P[:, 0], P[:, 1])
    return u, np.floor(y + 0.5)

s=s[:a]+'''    def near_sd(self, x, z):
        """the camera's own bank: a V opening onto the water (Ferrari's two foreground banks).
        Its edge is designed in the PICTURE (a composition line), then lifted onto the ground."""
        c, sn = np.cos(PITCH), np.sin(PITCH)
        yc = -HC * c + z * sn * -1 * -1 if False else None
        # camera coordinates of the ground point (x, 0, z)
        cy = -HC * c - z * sn * -1 if False else None
        # rotate world->camera (inverse pitch)
        Yw, Zw = -HC, z
        yc = Yw * c + Zw * sn
        zc = -Yw * sn + Zw * c
        zc = np.maximum(zc, 0.1)
        u = W / 2 + F * x / zc
        yscr = H / 2 - F * yc / zc
        s_ = 1.0 if self.bx < 0 else -1.0
        uu = u if s_ > 0 else (W - u)                      # uu: boulder side on the left
        n = self.noise
        wob = 5.0 * n.fbm2(uu / 23.0 + 40, np.full(np.shape(uu), 2.2), 3)
        e_left = H * 0.66 + (uu / (0.30 * W)) * (H * 0.40)           # low bank on the boulder side
        e_right = H + 4 - ((uu - 0.56 * W) / (0.44 * W)) * (H * 0.58)  # rising dark bank opposite
        edge = np.minimum(np.maximum(e_left, e_right), H + 6) + wob
        return (yscr - edge) * zc / F

'''+s[b:]

# output:
def ridge_raw(self, u):
        n = self.noise; ms = self.mtn_seed; uu = np.asarray(u, float) / W
        peak_u = 0.72 if self.bx < 0 else 0.28
        return 14 + 12 * np.exp(-((uu - peak_u) / 0.16) ** 2) + 5 * np.exp(-((uu - peak_u + 0.22) / 0.09) ** 2) \
            + 3.5 * n.fbm2(uu * 9 + ms, np.full(uu.shape, 1.7), 4) + 1.2 * np.abs(n.fbm2(uu * 40 + ms, np.full(uu.shape, 5.1), 2))

    def relief(self, u, v):
        n = self.noise; ms = self.mtn_seed
        return n.fbm2(u / 7.0 + ms, v / 4.0, 4) * 3.0 - np.abs(n.fbm2(u / 16.0 + ms, v / 9.0 + 3, 3)) * 7

    def backdrop(self, u, v):
        """material / value / aux for backdrop points. returns dict of arrays."""
        n = self.noise
        u = np.asarray(u, float); v = np.asarray(v, float)
        mat = np.zeros(u.shape, int)      # 0 sky, 1 mountain, 2 forest
        # sky: position along the violet->peach ramp. brightest/warmest near the horizon on the sun side
        az_sun = np.arctan2(self.l[0], self.l[2])
        az = np.arctan2((u - W / 2) / F, 1.0)
        warm = np.cos(az - az_sun)                           # 1 toward sun, -1 away
        e = np.clip(v / 60.0, 0, 1.5)
        pos = 3.9 - 3.3 * e ** 0.8 + 0.9 * warm * np.exp(-e * 1.5)
        if self.light == 'front':                            # sun behind camera: belt of Venus
            pos = 2.6 - 2.4 * np.clip(e, 0, 1) + 1.0 * np.exp(-((v - 12) / 9.0) ** 2) - 1.2 * np.exp(-(v / 3.5) ** 2)
        if self.light == 'back':                             # sun just above the frame: glow
            pos = pos + 0.9 * np.exp(-e * 1.0) * np.clip(warm, 0, 1) ** 4
        sky_pos = np.clip(pos, 0, 5.0)
        # mountain ridge (functions of u so derivatives are exact even for scattered samples)
        ridge = self.ridge(u)
        dr = self.ridge(u + 0.5) - self.ridge(u - 0.5)
        in_m = v < ridge
        gx = self.relief(u + 0.5, v) - self.relief(u - 0.5, v)
        sun_x = self.l[0]
        if self.light == 'back':
            facing = -0.5 + 0.0 * gx
        elif self.light == 'front':
            facing = 0.35 + 0.25 * np.abs(gx) - 0.25 * np.abs(dr)
        else:
            facing = -np.sign(sun_x) * (dr * 0.8 + gx * 0.6)
        snow = (v > ridge - 9 + 3 * n.fbm2(u / 5.0 + 9, v / 3.0, 3)) & (np.abs(dr) < 1.6) & in_m
        m_lit = facing > 0.15
        # forest (far shore) silhouettes
        top, trees = self._forest
        uc = np.clip(np.round(u).astype(int), 0, W - 1)
        f_top = top[uc]
        in_f = v < f_top
        f_lit = np.zeros(u.shape, bool)
        for (tx, hgt_t, wr) in trees:
            dy = hgt_t - v                                    # distance below the tip
            ok = (dy > 0) & (v > -0.5)
            if not ok.any(): continue
            tier = (np.floor(dy / 1.7) % 2)
            halfw = dy * wr * (1 + 0.35 * (((dy / 1.7) % 1.0) > 0.45)) + 0.35
            dx = u - tx
            inside = ok & (np.abs(dx) < halfw)
            in_f = in_f | inside
            side = dx * -np.sign(sun_x

    def relief(self, u, v):
        """depth-relief of the mountain face: big flanks either side of the peak + ridged spurs"""
        n = self.noise; ms = self.mtn_seed
        pu = (0.70 if self.bx < 0 else 0.30) * W
        spurs = 1 - np.abs(n.fbm2((u + 0.6 * v) / 13.0 + ms, (v - 0.3 * u) / 9.0, 3))
        spurs2 = 1 - np.abs(n.fbm2((u - 0.7 * v) / 6.0 + ms, v / 5.0 + 4, 2))
        return -0.35 * np.abs(u - pu) + 7 * spurs + 2.5 * spurs2

    def mountain_light(self, u, v):
        e = 0.7
        hu = (self.relief(u + e, v) - self.relief(u - e, v)) / (2 * e)
        hv = (self.relief(u, v + e) - self.relief(u, v - e)) / (2 * e)
        nrm = np.stack([-hu, 0.55 - 0.5 * hv, np.ones_like(hu)], -1)
        nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
        lm = np.array([self.l[0], self.l[1] + 0.12, -self.l[2]]); lm /= np.linalg.norm(lm)
        return (nrm * lm).sum(-1), nrm

def paint_strokes(self, img, kind, p, t):
        """grass blades & sedge, back to front, in counterchange with what lies behind"""
        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] + 101)
        out = img.copy()
        land = kind == 1
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(land)
        if len(ys) == 0: return out
        z = t[ys, xs]
        pl = p[ys, xs]
        # blade world height: short turf; sedge near the boulder foot and at the waterline
        db = self.boulder_sd(pl)
        sd = self.land_sd(pl[:, 0], pl[:, 2])
        near_b = np.exp(-np.maximum(db, 0) / (0.45 * self.r))
        clump = smoothstep(0.1, 0.6, self.noise.fbm2(pl[:, 0] * 0.9 + 3, pl[:, 2] * 0.9, 2))
        near_w = np.exp(-np.maximum(sd, 0) / 0.4) * clump
        hb = 0.07 + 0.45 * np.maximum(near_b, 0.7 * near_w) * self.r ** 0.3
        Lpx = F * hb / z
        dens = np.clip(0.10 + 0.5 * np.maximum(near_b, near_w), 0, 0.8) * (Lpx >= 1.5)
        spawn = rng.random(len(ys)) < dens
        order = np.argsort(ys[spawn], kind='stable')           # back (top) to front (bottom)
        bys, bxs, bL = ys[spawn][order], xs[spawn][order], Lpx[spawn][order]
        lit_here = self.land_lit.reshape(H, W)[bys, bxs] > 0.5
        lean_field = self.noise.fbm2(bxs / 23.0, bys / 17.0, 2, 9.1) * 0.55
        LVimg = LV
        sun_side = -np.sign(self.l[0]) if abs(self.l[0]) > 0.2 else 0
        for i in range(len(bys)):
            y0, x0 = bys[i], bxs[i]
            L = int(np.clip(round(bL[i] * rng.uniform(0.55, 1.25)), 1, 26))
            ang = lean_field[i] + rng.normal(0, 0.18)
            curl = rng.normal(0, 0.035)
            x = float(x0); y = y0
            bg = out[y0, x0]
            blit = lit_here[i]
            for k in range(L):
                yy = y - k
                if yy < 0: break
                xi = int(round(x))
                if not (0 <= xi < W): break
                behind = LVimg[out[yy, xi]]
                tip = k >= L - max(1, L // 4)
                # counterchange: dark blade over light, light blade over dark
                if behind > 42:
                    c = IDX['g2'] if (blit and rng.random() < 0.55) else IDX['g0']
                    if kind[yy, xi] == 2 or kind[yy, xi] == 0 or kind[yy, xi] == 3:
                        c = IDX['g0'] if behind > 55 else IDX['h1']
                else:
                    if blit:
                        c = IDX['g4'] if tip else IDX['g2']
                        if self.light == 'back' and tip: c = IDX['g5']
                    else:
                        c = IDX['h2'] if tip else (IDX['g1'] if behind > 16 else IDX['h1'])
                        if kind[yy, xi] != 1: c = IDX['sedge'] if tip else IDX['g1']
                out[yy, xi] = c
                ang += curl
                x += np.tan(np.clip(ang, -1.1, 1.1))
            # reflection of blades rooted at the waterline
            if near_w[spawn][order][i] > 0.6 and L >= 3:
                x = float(x0)

        # blade = straight runs with clean steps: a lower segment at one slope, the tip at a shallower
        # one (the arc of a sedge leaf), slopes from the pixel-clean set (dx per dy)
        SLOPES = [0.0, 1 / 3, 1 / 2, 1.0]
        for i in range(len(bys)):

                if behind > 42:                                     # on light: a dark blade...

                else:                                               # on dark: a light blade, lighter at the tip

                        c = IDX['h1'] if tipf < 0.35 else (IDX['g1'] if tipf < 0.7 else IDX['h2'])
                        if over_other: c = IDX['g1'] if tipf < 0.6 else IDX['sedge']
                    out[yy, xi] = c
                    if sun_dx and blit and 0 <= xi - sun_dx < W and tipf > 0.2 and not over_other:
                        out[yy, xi - sun_dx] = IDX['g0']                # its shadow side
            # reflection of blades rooted at the waterline: short, dark, broken
            if wline[i] > 0.6 and L >= 3:
                for (yy, xi, k) in pts[:max(1, int(len(pts) * 0.55))]:

                    if ry < H and kind[ry, xi] == 3 and rng.random() > 0.25:
                        out[ry, xi] = IDX['for0'] if LV[out[ry, xi]] > 30 else IDX['g1']

"""Apply the SAME analyses used on Ferrari to painted cells, and compare side by side.
usage: python src/check_sheets.py <cell_key> [<cell_key> ...]   (keys as in sheets/cells/*.png)"""
import sys, os, json
sys.path.insert(0, 'src')
from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from collections import Counter
import matplotlib; matplotlib.use('Agg'); import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from clusters import cluster_stats, run_lengths
from dither import dither_stats
from values import analyse
from depth import depth_stats, plot as depth_plot
from planes_names import NAMES as FNAMES

CELLS = 'sheets/cells'
MY = ['sky', 'mountain', 'far forest', 'water', 'bank (boulder side)', 'boulder', 'near bank lit', 'near bank shade']
# correspondences: my plane -> Ferrari plane ids
CORR = {'sky': [0], 'mountain': [1], 'far forest': [2], 'water': [6], 'bank (boulder side)': [7, 5],
        'boulder': [1], 'near bank lit': [8], 'near bank shade': [9]}


def load_cell(key):
    a = np.array(Image.open(f'{CELLS}/{key}.png').convert('RGB'))
    pl = np.load(f'{CELLS}/{key}_planes.npy')
    # split the near bank into its lit and shaded masses by squinted value
    Lb = gaussian_filter(lum(a), 3)
    p2 = pl.copy()
    near = pl == 6
    p2[near & (Lb < 24)] = 7
    return a, p2


def stats_table(a, P, names, corr_ref=None):
    rows = {}
    lab = rgb2lab(a); L = lab[..., 0]
    same_v = np.all(a[1:] == a[:-1], axis=2); same_h = np.all(a[:, 1:] == a[:, :-1], axis=2)
    ds, _ = dither_stats(a, P, names)
    for i, n in enumerate(names):
        m = P == i
        if m.sum() < 80: continue
        cs = cluster_stats(a, m)
        c = Counter(map(tuple, a[m])); tot = sum(c.values())
        cum = np.cumsum(sorted(c.values(), reverse=True)) / tot
        rows[n] = dict(area=float(m.mean()), L_med=float(np.median(gaussian_filter(L, 2.5)[m])),
                       L_p5=float(np.percentile(L[m], 5)), L_p95=float(np.percentile(L[m], 95)),
                       colours_90=int(np.searchsorted(cum, 0.9) + 1), vsame=float(same_v[m[1:]].mean()),
                       hsame=float(same_h[m[:, 1:]].mean()), cluster_med=float(cs['size_med']),
                       cluster_mean=float(cs['size_mean']), single=float(cs['single']),
                       checker=float(ds.get(n, {}).get('checker', 0)), chroma=float(np.median(np.hypot(lab[..., 1], lab[..., 2])[m])),
                       b=float(np.median(lab[..., 2][m])))
    return rows


if __name__ == '__main__':
    keys = sys.argv[1:]
    ref = load_ref(); Pref = np.load('out/planes.npy')
    R = stats_table(ref, Pref, FNAMES)
    allrows = {'ferrari': R}
    for key in keys:
        a, P = load_cell(key)
        res, _ = analyse(a, P, MY, key, f'chk_{key}')
        M = stats_table(a, P, MY)
        allrows[key] = M
        rows = depth_stats(a, P, [('near bank', [6, 7]), ('bank (boulder side)', [4]), ('boulder', [5]),
                                  ('far forest', [2]), ('mountain', [1]), ('sky', [0])])
        depth_plot(rows, f'img/chk_{key}_depth.png', f'{key}: depth cues, near -> far')
        # side-by-side notan / grey with Ferrari (reference scaled to the same height)
        for kind in ('notan', 'grey'):
            mine = np.array(Image.open(f'img/chk_{key}_{kind}.png').convert('RGB'))
            theirs = np.array(Image.open(f'img/03_{kind}.png').convert('RGB'))
            h = min(mine.shape[0], theirs.shape[0])
            th = np.array(Image.fromarray(theirs).resize((int(theirs.shape[1] * h / theirs.shape[0]), h), Image.NEAREST))
            mi = np.array(Image.fromarray(mine).resize((int(mine.shape[1] * h / mine.shape[0]), h), Image.NEAREST))
            Image.fromarray(np.concatenate([th, np.full((h, 10, 3), 255, np.uint8), mi], 0 if kind == 'notan' else 1)).save(f'img/chk_{key}_{kind}_vs.png')
    json.dump(allrows, open('out/check_stats.json', 'w'), indent=1)
    # print comparison of corresponding planes
    for key in keys:
        print('=' * 20, key)
        for n, ids in CORR.items():
            if n not in allrows[key]: continue
            m = allrows[key][n]; f = allrows['ferrari'][FNAMES[ids[0]]]
            print(f'{n:20s} vs {FNAMES[ids[0]]:18s}| ' + ' '.join(f'{k}={m[k]:.2f}/{f[k]:.2f}' for k in ('L_med', 'L_p5', 'L_p95', 'colours_90', 'vsame', 'hsame', 'cluster_mean', 'single', 'checker', 'b')))

# Learning to paint like Mark Ferrari: a study of *Alpine Tarn at Dusk*

Everything below comes from programs in `src/`, and every figure is in `img/`. I looked at each
figure myself before writing about it. The reference arrived as a 1268×938 PNG. It is an exact 2×
nearest-neighbour upscale (every 2×2 block is one colour, with no phase error), so the native
picture is **634×469 in 172 colours** (`img/00_native.png`). All measurements below are made on
the native pixels.

Contents

1. Analysis, drawn: planes, values, light, form, edges, clusters, palette, depth, pixel geometry
2. What it means: Ferrari's method, as a painter would teach it
3. The method: `src/paint/ferrari_method.py`, parameters and sheets
4. Checking my sheets with the same analyses, one by one
5. What I would do next

---

## 1. The analysis

### 1.1 Planes and big masses (`img/02_planes.png`)

I traced ten planes by hand, using polygons for the ground and colour tests to split sky from tree
and rock. Share of the picture:

| plane | area | | plane | area |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sky | 17.9% | | water | 16.5% |
| mountain | 4.0% | | left meadow (mid) | 4.6% |
| far forest | 9.0% | | left bank (fore) | 10.8% |
| framing trees | 15.7% | | right bank (fore) | 13.1% |
| far banks / hummocks | 6.6% | | island | 1.8% |

The composition is a **V of foreground banks opening onto water**. The lit left bank drops from the
middle-left down to the bottom centre, and the shadowed right bank rises diagonally from the bottom
centre to the right edge. The water sits in the V. Above it a band of low hummocks carries the far
shore, then a dark forest band, a small peak and a large sky. Tall framing conifers on both sides
close the rectangle. The peak, the only warm-and-cool jewel, sits almost dead centre, in a notch
the conifers leave open. Its reflection lies exactly under it, inside the V, so the V points the
eye at the mirrored peak.

### 1.2 Value structure (`img/03_grey.png`, `img/03_notan.png`, `img/03_valueplanes.png`, `img/03_valueplan.png`)

The grey version uses CIE L\*. Beside it is a squint version (Gaussian σ=2.5 px), because dithering
means individual pixels lie about the value a region reads as.

* **Two-value notan** (cut at L\*≈40): light = sky + snowfield + the reflected sky in the water +
  the lit left bank. Dark = every tree, the far banks and the whole right bank. It reads as an
  hourglass. A light field at the top pours through the notch around the peak into a light field
  at the bottom (water plus the left bank), pinched by two dark masses of trees and banks.
* **Three-value notan**, with k-means centres at L\* **16 / 42 / 70**. Every plane stays inside one
  or two of the three groups (squint median, then the 5–95% spread):

| plane | median | 5–95% | group |
|---|---|---|---|
| sky | 71 | 45–75 (the spread is only the tree edges) | light |
| mountain | 48 | 17–72 | all three: the one place that uses the full range |
| far forest | 20 | 9–44 | dark |
| framing trees | 16 | 5–40 | dark |
| far banks | 18 | 10–28 | dark |
| island | 28 | 16–52 | dark, with a mid rim |
| water | 29 | 14–70 | mirrors whatever is above it |
| left meadow (mid) | 42 | 22–55 | middle |
| left bank (fore) | 43 | 28–51 | middle |
| right bank (fore) | 14 | 4–22 | dark |

* **Nothing is white.** The lightest sizeable mass is the sky at L\* 71–79. The only pixels above 80
  are a few lit snow and alpenglow crest pixels on the peak, so **the peak carries the value
  extreme**. The darks go all the way to pure black (0,0,0), which is 19% of all pixels. Every one
  of them is in the trees and the near banks.
* **The ordering follows Carlson's angle theory of landscape values.** The sky is the source and the
  lightest (71). Flat planes are next, taking the most skylight: the lit meadow and the water
  reflecting the sky (42–70). Slanted planes come after them: the mountain and the mound faces.
  Uprights are darkest: the trees (16–20). The right bank breaks the rule only because it is in
  cast shadow.

### 1.3 Light (`img/05_lightmap.png`, `img/05_light_mountain.png`, `img/05_light_tree.png`, `img/07_profiles.png`)

I classed every pixel as deep shadow, shadow, cool light, warm lit or warm highlight, using value
plus warmth (b\*>12).

* **The sun is low, on the left and slightly behind the scene.** The evidence: the lit faces of
  the peak are the ones facing left, with a 1-px salmon line running along the left side of each
  crest (`05_light_mountain`). The conifers' ochre highlights sit on the upper-left of each branch
  tier. The far hummocks and the island are **rim-lit on top, with dark faces toward us** (the
  island's value profile peaks just under its crest at L\* 52 and falls to 27 at its base).
* **The sky has no sun in it.** Across the frame the sky is flat (L\* 73.5–74.7 in every column).
  Top to bottom it is an almost **iso-luminant hue sweep**: violet at the top (b\* −8.7, L\* 71.5)
  to peach at the horizon (b\* +21, L\* 79). **In this palette, colour changes and value does not.**
* **Warm light, cool shade, with a hard rule per material.** Sunlit ochre grass sits against black
  and olive shadow. Salmon and peach lit rock sits against violet shadow rock. Peach lit snow sits
  against lavender shadow snow. Shade gets its colour from the sky (violet) or from the local
  colour (olive), never from the sun.
* **Cast shadows from things outside the frame organise the ground.** The whole right foreground
  bank lies in shadow, and so do the island's left half and the far-left hummock's face. There is
  nothing in the frame to cast these shadows. They are compositional (a repoussoir), and they are
  justified by the forest off-frame.
* **Ground planes brighten as they recede.** The left bank goes from L\* ~30–45 at the bottom to 53
  at its far edge, and the right bank from 5 to 20 (`07_profiles`). Two things are behind this: the
  sheen of grass seen at a grazing angle toward the light, and deliberate darkening of the frame's
  bottom and corners (a vignette that funnels the eye inward).

### 1.4 Form (`img/07_form.png`, `img/07_form_mid.png`, `img/07_profiles.png`)

The overlay draws isophotes of the squint value inside each mass, with arrows pointing up the
brightness gradient (toward the lit side).

* **Hummocks and the island are lozenges.** A domed top, a flat bottom, ends tapered to points. The
  light sits in a band just below the crest, then value falls off down the front toward a dark
  base. A thin dark accent sits on top of the crest where the rim meets the darker things behind.
  The bottoms meet the water in a dead-level line (1.9).
* **The peak is planes, not gradients.** Each facet is one value, lit or shade, and the facets are
  separated by crisp edges or by 1–3 px checker strips. Snow is **large flat shapes** with jagged,
  hard-edged rock outcrops cut into them.
* **The banks are tilted planes.** They are not modelled at all. They are one value mass with
  texture, graded only by distance.
* **The trees are silhouettes plus flecks.** A black spire; horizontal, drooping branch spurs
  1 px thick; ochre flecks on the sun side of each tier.

### 1.5 Edges (`img/08_edges.png`)

I found mass edges on a median-filtered (de-dithered) image with Canny at σ=3.5, then measured the
20→80% rise width along each edge's normal. Red = hard (≤2 px), yellow = mid, blue = soft (>4.5 px).

* **The hard, found edges are silhouettes against the sky**: the conifers (50% of sky edges are
  hard, median contrast 69 L), the crest of the peak, and the bottom edge of the reflected mountain
  against the reflected sky.
* **The soft edges are all interior modelling.** Rock facets inside the mountain, the tree
  interiors, and the way the grass meets the grass (58–64% of bank edges are soft).
* **Lost edges** (next-to-no contrast between neighbouring planes, measured as ΔL across the
  boundary):
  * the far banks against their own reflections (ΔL 0.3), so the far shoreline is only a thin line;
  * the right bank against the water, where dark grass silhouettes break the line;
  * the mountain's right shoulder melting into the sky (ΔL 5 over the whole boundary);
  * framing trees against the far forest (ΔL 2.5).

  Ferrari lets the dark masses merge into one another and keeps the found edges for dark against
  light.

### 1.6 Cluster shapes (`img/09_cluster_atlas.png`, `img/11_checker_map.png`, magnified crops `img/c_*.png`)

Each atlas tile is a 32×32 window at 7×, with its three dominant colours isolated beside it.

| material | cluster vocabulary | measured |
|---|---|---|
| **near grass, lit** | 1-px vertical strokes 3–10 px tall. Mid-brown strokes and black-green gaps on a lit ochre base | **4 colours cover 90%**: base L60 38%, mid stroke L40 35%, olive L24 11%, gap L10 12%. Vertical neighbour same colour 70%, horizontal 34% |
| **near grass, shade** | *Negative painting*: olive strokes 1 px wide, curved and diagonal, on black | 4 colours cover 90%: black and near-black 59%, olive 40% |
| **mid meadow** | the strokes shrink to 1×2 ticks, then to irregular speckle. Speckle density *is* the value gradient | 7 colours cover 90% |
| **far banks** | near-uniform random dither of 2–3 close dark values: texture without strokes | |
| **island top** | lit band with a serrated, 1-px grass-tip top edge; value falls off through dither density | 14% checker |
| **conifer** | black mass, 1–3 px ochre flecks along drooping diagonal branch lines, sky holes | **3 colours cover 95%**: black 61%, ochre 22%, dark 14% |
| **conifer edge** | stair-stepped horizontal spurs, alternating left and right, longer lower down | |
| **mountain rock** | diagonal strokes along the fall line, 1-px crest lines, checker between violets | 15 colours for 90% |
| **snow** | flat areas; crisp jagged outcrops | |
| **sky** | **flat bands 6–10 rows tall, joined by 1–3 rows of checkerboard** | 8 colours cover 90%. 24.5% of 2×2 blocks are checkers |
| **water, sky reflection** | horizontal rows of checker; 1-px horizontal ripple lines | |
| **water, tree reflection** | broken speckle; ochre fragments | 44 colours cover 80%: the water holds every colour in the picture |
| **shore stones** | flat horizontal lozenges: light top row, mid body, dark bottom row, 45° ends | |

Two rules sit above the table. **Every material has a stroke direction**: grass is vertical,
conifer branches horizontal and drooping, rock diagonal along the slope, water horizontal. And
**the texture scale shrinks with distance** (strokes, then ticks, then speckle, then flat). The
colour count per plane is inverted from what you might expect. The nearest, most-textured planes
use **3–4 colours**. Complexity in colour lives only where the picture needs *light*: the peak
(15 colours) and the water (44).

### 1.7 Palette (`img/10_palette_groups.png`, `img/10_palette_ab.png`)

I assigned every colour to the material where it is used most:

* **The sky** (11 colours): L\* 71–76, hue sweeping violet 331° to peach 42°. It is an iso-luminant
  ramp.
* **The mountain** (17): a violet shadow ramp (L 36–44) plus salmon and peach lights (L 68–77), and
  lavender snow.
* **Conifers** (18): black, 5–6 dark violets that stand for distance, one ochre highlight (136,101,50).
* **Lit grass** (17): black-green, olive, then brown to ochre (196,131,70). The two dominant ochres
  differ by only (0,3,6).
* **Shade grass** (16): black-green through dull olive, with low chroma.
* **Water-only** (76): darkened and cooled copies of everything, mostly mauves.

In a\*b\* space the palette has **two hue families**. Sun colours are yellow-ochre and orange with
b\* +15..+45. Sky and air colours are violet-pink with b\* −15..+20. Dark olives are the only green
(a\*<0), and they are low in value. **Chroma peaks at mid values (L 40–70) and collapses in the
darks**: the most saturated colours are the lit ochre, the salmon crest and the peach glow, which
are exactly where the sun lands.

### 1.8 Depth (`img/12_depth.png`)

| plane (near → far) | squint L | darkest (p3) | chroma | b\* |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| near banks | 21 | **0** | 23 | +22 |
| framing trees | 16 | **0** | — | — |
| left meadow | 42 | 10 | 28 | +27 |
| island | 28 | 3 | 18 | +17 |
| far banks | 18 | 0 | 8 | +8 |
| far forest | 20 | 0 | 8 | 0 |
| **mountain** | 48 | **36** | 19 | **−5** (a\* +16) |
| sky | 71 | 71 | 22 | +1 |

Depth is made by, in order of strength:

1. **Overlap.** Seven layers stacked, with the V banks overlapping the water.
2. **The black point rises with distance.** Black up to the far forest, then a jump to L\* 36 for
   the peak: atmosphere is modelled as *lifted darks* more than as lowered lights.
3. **Hue.** Yellow-ochre near, grey-olive in the middle, violet-pink far.
4. **Texture scale.** Strokes, ticks, speckle, flat.
5. **Chroma** falls in the middle distance.

### 1.9 Pixel geometry: runs, steps and the bottoms of masses (`src/contours.py`, `img/c_island_bottom.png`, `img/c_hummock_base.png`, `img/c_stones.png`)

I ran this pass after a note from David (the critic): the medium favours short straight runs and
clean 45° and 2:1 steps. I measured how Ferrari's silhouettes break into runs:

| contour | 1:1 steps | 2:1 | flat run of 3–6 then one step | drop of ≥3 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| mountain crest (left) | 41% | 3% | **53%** | 0 |
| mountain right shoulder | 43% | 29% | 5% | 14% |
| conifer line | 18% | 12% | 1% | 46% (the vertical spires) |

**The crest has no noise.** It is straight segments, each with one consistent step pattern: a run
of 1:1 steps, then a run of 4-4-4 flats, then 2:1. The magnified bottoms show a second rule:
**masses meet level ground or water in a dead-flat horizontal run**, with a 1-px darker contact
line and horizontal streaks right at the join. The island is a lozenge on a flat base. The shore
stones have flat tops and bottoms, 45° ends, and **light top row, dark bottom row: two values in a
single stroke.** Hummock flanks cross the ground in clean 2:1 diagonals.

---

## 2. What it means: the method as a painter would teach it

**Plan the picture in three values, then keep each mass inside its group.** Ferrari's picture works
in squint: light sky pouring down into light water and a lit bank, clamped by two dark masses.
Every plane stays in one value group (1.2). That is why an image of 172 colours and a million
dither pixels reads instantly. The texture is huge, but it is *confined* to a value band.

**Value is the structure. Colour is temperature and distance.** The sky is one value in eleven
colours. The lit grass is four values in four colours. When he needs to show light he spends hue
and chroma (warm) against cool, lower-chroma shade. When he needs to show distance he lifts the
black point and turns violet.

**Contrast is impressionistic and happens inside the stroke.** The strongest contrasts are not
between masses. They are inside each small mark:

* a grass blade is a light stroke with a black gap beside it;
* a conifer tier is an ochre fleck on black;
* a crest is a salmon line on violet;
* a stone is a light top row on a dark bottom row.

Across the picture, the mass values are held close. Up close, every stroke carries its own
light-dark pair. So the picture is calm at a distance and vibrant up close, like an
Impressionist's broken colour.

**The strokes are the material.** Grass is vertical strokes, water is horizontal rows, rock is
diagonal fall lines, conifers are horizontal drooping spurs. Direction alone tells you what a
thing is, before colour does. Texture shrinks with distance, so the stroke grammar itself is
perspective.

**Spend detail and colour where the light is.** The foreground is 3–4 colours of heavy texture.
The water and the peak get all the colours, because that is where light happens.

**Use the medium's own geometry.** Silhouettes are straight runs with regular steps. Ground
contacts are flat. The pixel grid is embraced as a drawing language, not hidden, so shapes look
*designed* rather than sampled.

**Find edges against the light, lose them in the dark.** Silhouettes against sky and water are
hard. Dark meets dark with no line at all. The eye is led by where edges are found.

**Organise light with shadows from outside the frame**, and **darken the corners**. The dark right
bank and the lit left bank are not physics. They are the composition's framing, and they are
plausible because of the forest.

**Reflections: exact mirror, compressed range.** The mirror axis is the far shoreline (row 228, at
scale 1.00). The reflected sky is ~4 L\* darker than the sky, the brightest lights drop by ~13 L\*,
and darks stay put (+1). Colours shift slightly cooler (a\* −5). Horizontal checker and streak
lines say *water* without ever breaking the mirror. Here too is the colour-cycling legacy. In his
256-colour scenes Ferrari reserved palette rows for cycling gradients (water, rain, fog), laid
them with stencils, and ran several gradient lengths and speeds at once so the motion never pulses
in step (Q&A with Mark J. Ferrari, EffectGames; his GDC 2016 talk *8 Bit & '8 Bitish'
Graphics — Outside the Box*). The horizontal streaks in this tarn are exactly the kind of rows he
would cycle. He dithered patterned where he wanted precision (the sky seams and the water
checkers) and diffusion or chaotic where he wanted texture (the grass and foam) (same source).

        45-degree ends, a dark reflection line under them (Ferrari's shore stones)"""

        out = img.copy()
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(kind == 3)
        if len(ys) == 0: return out
        pw = p[ys, xs]
        sd = self.land_sd(pw[:, 0], pw[:, 2])
        z = t[ys, xs]

        cand = np.flatnonzero(ok)

        for j in rng.choice(cand, n, replace=False):

                        out[yy, xx] = col
            yy = y + 1
            for xx in range(x - w // 2 + 1, x + w // 2):
                if 0 <= yy < H and 0 <= xx < W and kind[yy, xx] == 3 and rng.random() < 0.8:
                    out[yy, xx] = IDX['w0']
        return out

        rng = np.random.default_rng(self.p['seed'] * 7 + 3)

                    jit = np.stack([(h % 97) / 97, ((h // 97) % 89) / 89, ((h // 8633) % 83) / 83], 1)
                    dd = np.linalg.norm(q - (cc + jit), axis=1)
                    better = dd < best
                    best = np.where(better, dd, best); cid = np.where(better, h, cid)
        uniq, inv = np.unique(cid, return_inverse=True)

        mean_n /= np.linalg.norm(mean_n, axis=1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9

Bash: Show march function
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && sed -n 285,335p src/paint/ferrari_method.py

# output:
def march(self, o, d, tmax=260.0):
        """march rays; returns t, kind (0 none, 1 land, 2 boulder, 3 water)"""
        N = d.shape[0]
        t = np.full(N, 0.05); kind = np.zeros(N, int); alive = np.ones(N, bool)
        # water plane
        tw = np.where(d[:, 1] < -1e-6, -o[:, 1] / np.minimum(d[:, 1], -1e-6), np.inf)
        prev_t = t.copy()
        for it in range(420):
            idx = np.flatnonzero(alive)
            if idx.size == 0: break
            p = o[idx] + d[idx] * t[idx, None]
            sb = self.boulder_sd(p)
            hb = self.land_h(p[:, 0], p[:, 2])
            below = p[:, 1] < hb
            hitb = sb < 0.004 * max(1.0, t[idx].mean() * 0.02)
            # land hit (bisection refine later coarse is fine at these scales)
            hl = below & (hb > -0.001)
            hw = t[idx] >= tw[idx]
            done = hitb | hl | hw | (t[idx] > tmax)
            kind[idx[hitb]] = 2
            kl = idx[hl & ~hitb]; kind[kl] = 1
            kw = idx[hw & ~hitb & ~hl]; kind[kw] = 3; t[kw] = tw[kw]
            # refine land hits by bisection between prev_t and t
            if kl.size:
                a, b = prev_t[kl], t[kl]
                for _ in range(8):
                    m = (a + b) / 2; pm = o[kl] + d[kl] * m[:, None]
                    inside = pm[:, 1] < self.land_h(pm[:, 0], pm[:, 2])
                    b = np.where(inside, m, b); a = np.where(inside, a, m)
                t[kl] = b
            alive[idx[done]] = False
            rem = idx[~done]
            if rem.size == 0: break
            step = np.minimum(np.abs(sb[~done]) * 0.9 + 0.002, 0.02 + t[rem] * 0.012)
            prev_t[rem] = t[rem]
            t[rem] = t[rem] + np.maximum(step, 0.004)
        kind[(kind == 0) & np.isfinite(tw)] = 3
        t = np.where((kind == 3), np.minimum(t, tw), t)
        return t, kind

    def boulder_shadow(self, p):
        """soft visibility of the sun from points p (boulder occludes; low sun -> long shadows)"""
        vis = np.ones(p.shape[0]); t = np.full(p.shape[0], 0.03)
        for _ in range(80):
            q = p + self.l[None] * t[:, None]
            s = self.boulder_sd(q)
            vis = np.minimum(vis, np.clip(10 * s / t, 0, 1))
            t = t + np.clip(s, 0.02, 0.5 * self.r)
            if (t > 12 * self.r).all(): break
        return vis

        grng = np.random.default_rng(seed + 29)
        r = self.r; d = self.bz

b='''    def tree_sd(self, p, with_id=False):
        """conifers: tiered cones (drooping branch tiers, spiky spurs) on short trunks"""
        best = np.full(p.shape[:-1], 1e9); tid = np.full(p.shape[:-1], -1)
        for i, (tx, tz, h, rb) in enumerate(self.trees):
            y0 = self.land_h(np.array([tx]), np.array([tz]))[0].clip(0, None) - 0.2
            dx = p[..., 0] - tx; dz = p[..., 2] - tz
            rr = np.sqrt(dx * dx + dz * dz)
            yy = p[..., 1] - y0
            fr = np.clip(yy / h, 0, 1)
            tier_len = 1.1 + 0.05 * h
            tier = ((h - yy) / tier_len) % 1.0                    # 0 at a tier's top, 1 at its drooping skirt
            ang = np.arctan2(dz, dx)
            spur = 0.75 + 0.45 * np.abs(np.sin(ang * 3.5 + yy * 1.7 + i))
            R = rb * (1 - fr) * (0.55 + 0.6 * tier) * spur * (yy > 1.2)
            dcone = (rr - R) * 0.45
            dcone = np.where(yy > h, np.hypot(rr, yy - h), dcone)
            dcone = np.where(yy < 0, np.maximum(dcone, -yy), dcone)
            trunk = np.maximum(rr - 0.18, np.maximum(-yy, yy - 2.0))
            dd = np.minimum(dcone, trunk)
            better = dd < best
            best = np.where(better, dd, best); tid = np.where(better, i, tid)
        return (best, tid) if with_id else best

    def boulder_normal(self, p):'''

a="""    def paint_boulder(self, img, Lt, kind, p, d, T):"""
b='''    def tree_look(self, pt, T_rand, yscr, u):
        """palette index for conifer points: black mass, ochre flecks on the sun side of each
        drooping tier (the reference: black 61%, ochre 22%, dark 14% -- 3 colours)"""
        _, tid = self.tree_sd(pt, with_id=True)
        tr = np.array(self.trees)[np.clip(tid, 0, None)]
        tx, tz, h, rb = tr[:, 0], tr[:, 1], tr[:, 2], tr[:, 3]
        dx = pt[:, 0] - tx; dz = pt[:, 2] - tz
        rr = np.sqrt(dx ** 2 + dz ** 2) + 1e-6
        nrm = np.stack([dx / rr, np.full_like(rr, 0.45), dz / rr], 1); nrm /= np.linalg.norm(nrm, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        ls = (nrm * self.l).sum(1)
        zc = np.maximum(pt[:, 2], 0.5)
        u_t = W / 2 + F * tx / zc
        tier_px = np.maximum(2.0, F * (1.1 + 0.05 * h) / zc)
        stripe = ((yscr - 0.55 * np.abs(u - u_t)) / tier_px) % 1.0
        col = np.where(T_rand < 0.16, IDX['for0'], IDX['k'])
        fleck = (ls > 0.05) & (stripe < 0.42) & (T_rand > 0.25)
        if self.light == 'back':
            fleck = (ls > 0.55) & (stripe < 0.3) & (T_rand > 0.5)
        col = np.where(fleck, IDX['g2'], col)
        col = np.where(fleck & (ls > 0.55) & (T_rand > 0.8), IDX['g3'], col)
        return col

    def paint_trees(self, img, kind, p, d, T):
        m = kind == 4
        self.tree_mask = m.reshape(H, W)
        if not m.any(): return
        yy, xx = np.divmod(np.flatnonzero(m), W)
        col = self.tree_look(p[m], T['speckle'][yy, xx], yy.astype(float), xx.astype(float))
        img[m] = col
        if self.light == 'back':                 # rim light on the silhouette against the glowing sky
            tm = self.tree_mask
            edge = tm & ~(np.roll(tm, 1, 0) & np.roll(tm, 1, 1) & np.roll(tm, -1, 1) & np.roll(tm, -1, 0))
            e = edge.ravel()[m]
            img[m] = np.where(e & (T['speckle'][yy, xx] > 0.35), IDX['g4'], img[m])

    def paint_boulder(self, img, Lt, kind, p, d, T):'''

a=s.index("        # texture spread: near grass spans base / mid stroke / dark gap"); b=s.index("        use_lit = lit_amt > T['vert'][yy, xx] * 0.8 + 0.1")
s=s[:a]+'''        # NEAR grass: Ferrari's measured recipe, a base colour broken by strokes in fixed proportions
        # (lit: base L60 38% / mid stroke L40 35% / olive L24 11% / gap L10 12%;
        #  shade: black 28% / black-green 31% / olive 40%). The stroke threshold map is vertical, so the
        # proportions come out as vertical 1-px strokes; the plane's value shifts the thresholds.
        sh_l = np.clip((L_lit - 50) / 40, -0.3, 0.3)
        c_lit = np.select([Tv < 0.12 - sh_l * 0.3, Tv < 0.23 - sh_l * 0.4, Tv < 0.58 - sh_l],
                          [IDX['g0'], IDX['g1'], IDX['g2']], np.where(L_lit > 57, IDX['g4'], IDX['g3']))
        sh_s = np.clip((L_sh - 12) / 30, -0.3, 0.3)
        c_sh = np.select([Tv < 0.28 - sh_s, Tv < 0.60 - sh_s], [IDX['k'], IDX['g0']],
                         np.where(Tv > 0.9 - sh_s * 0.5, IDX['h2'], IDX['g1']))
        # FAR grass: the spread shrinks to a two-colour speckle around the plane's value
        amp = 6
        Tq = T['checker'][yy, xx]
        far_lit = ramp_pick('grass_lit', L_lit + amp * (Ts * 2 - 1), Tq, 0.3)
        far_sh = ramp_pick('grass_shade', L_sh + amp * (Ts * 2 - 1), Tq, 0.3)
        nearm = near > T['speckle'][yy, xx] * 0.6 + 0.2
        lit_i = np.where(nearm, c_lit, far_lit)
        sh_i = np.where(nearm, c_sh, far_sh)
'''+s[b:]
rep=[("        lit_amt = smoothstep(-0.32, -0.12, ndl) * smoothstep(0.35, 0.8, vis)","        lit_amt = smoothstep(-0.5, -0.3, ndl) * smoothstep(0.35, 0.8, vis)"),
("    T['horiz'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2(xx / 7.0, yy / 0.7, 2, 2.3) + 0.25 * rng.random((H, W)))","    T['horiz'] = rank_uniform(noise.fbm2(xx / 8.0, yy / 1.1, 2, 2.3) + 0.10 * rng.random((H, W)))"),
("        for k in range(grng.integers(3, 6)):","        for k in range(grng.integers(5, 9)):"),
("        n_t = int(grng.integers(3, 6))\n        xs_ = self.bx - A * (0.5 + np.sort(grng.uniform(0, 1.3, n_t))) - 1.0",
 "        n_t = int(grng.integers(5, 9))\n        xs_ = self.bx - A * (0.4 + np.sort(grng.uniform(0, 2.0, n_t))) - 1.0"),
]
for a_,b_ in rep:
    if a_ in s: s=s.replace(a_,b_)
    else: print('skip', a_[:50])
open(f,'w').write(s)

Bash: Run checks on all 19 cells and summarise medians vs Ferrari
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/pedagogy/analysis && ls sheets/cells/*.png | sed 's|sheets/cells/||; s|\.png||' | tr '\n' ' ' > out/cellkeys.txt; nice -n 10 python3 src/check_sheets.py $(cat out/cellkeys.txt) > out/check_all.txt 2>&1; tail -3 out/check_all.txt; nice -n 10 python3 - <<'EOF'
import json, numpy as np
d=json.load(open('out/check_stats.json'))
F=d.pop('ferrari')
CORR={'sky':'sky','mountain':'mountain','far forest':'far forest','water':'water','bank (boulder side)':'left meadow (mid)','boulder':'mountain','near bank lit':'left bank (fore)','near bank shade':'right bank (fore)','grove':'framing trees'}
keys=['L_med','L_p5','L_p95','colours_90','vsame','hsame','cluster_mean','single','checker','b']
print(f"{'plane':20s} {'Ferrari plane':18s} "+' '.join(f'{k:>13s}' for k in keys))
rows=[]
for n,fn in CORR.items():
    vals=[c[n] for c in d.values() if n in c]
    if not vals: continue
    m={k:np.median([v[k] for v in vals]) for k in keys}
    lo={k:np.percentile([v[k] for v in vals],10) for k in keys}; hi={k:np.percentile([v[k] for v in vals],90) for k in keys}
    print(f"{n:20s} {fn:18s} "+' '.join(f"{m[k]:6.2f}/{F[fn][k]:6.2f}" for k in keys), f"(n={len(vals)})")
    rows.append((n,fn,m,lo,hi,len(vals)))
json.dump([(n,fn,m,lo,hi,c) for n,fn,m,lo,hi,c in rows],open('out/check_summary.json','w'),indent=1)
EOF

---

## 3. The method (`src/paint/ferrari_method.py`, `src/paint/pixelgeo.py`, `src/paint/sheets.py`)

`paint(size, light, distance, seed)` returns a 320×180 image in a fixed 32-colour palette, and
`python src/paint/sheets.py` writes the sheets to `sheets/`. Every cell is also saved at native
size in `sheets/cells/`, with its palette-index map and plane map, so the cells can be analysed.
Parameters:

* `size`: small, medium or large. The boulder radius is 0.55, 0.95 or 1.45 m (1.1 / 1.9 / 2.9 m
  across).
* `light`: left, front, right or back. The sun stays about 7° up. Front means the sun is behind the
  viewer and slightly left, so the form still turns.
* `distance`: close, 10m or 40m. Close is 4.5 m.
* `seed`: varies the shoreline, boulder weathering, trees, mountain, blades and stones.

The camera is 1.1 m above the water, looking 10° down, with a 50° horizontal field of view. So the
horizon sits at row 29.5 and the frame's top edge is only 4.5° above horizontal. That drove a
design decision taken straight from the analysis. **Everything tall (the peak and the conifers) is
taller than the frame. The frame crops it, and the water shows it whole.** In the reference the
reflected peak is the thing the V of banks points to. Here the reflected peak and the reflected
conifers fill the water.

### The pipeline, with the analysis finding behind each step

1. **Geometry, as a G-buffer.** I ray-march:
   * a weathered, lumpy ellipsoid for the boulder, about 35% sunk;
   * a heightfield of banks: the boulder's promontory, whose shoreline wraps the boulder's
     front-right so its reflection hangs straight below it, a hummock behind it, and a far-right
     hummock;
   * tiered-cone conifers;
   * the water plane.

   The mountain, sky and far forest are a backdrop, defined in picture coordinates (column, rows
   above the horizon) so the reflection can sample them at the mirrored elevation.

   *Found in 1.1:* the V of foreground banks. The near bank is designed **in the picture plane** as
   two composition lines, then lifted onto the ground. A low lit bank sits on the boulder side and
   a bank rises diagonally, dark, on the other side. The V opens wider when the camera is farther
   away, so the foreground is never empty.
2. **Value first.** Each material computes a target L\*, and the scale is taken from the
   reference's value groups:
   * sky 71–79;
   * peak darks lifted to ≥37;
   * lit grass about 48–60 and shade grass 10–20;
   * boulder: light 44–78, core shadow down to 13;
   * water = the source value, compressed (1.2, 2).
3. **Colour second.** The value is looked up in the material's ramp: a warm ramp in light and a
   cool ramp in shade. There is never a nearest-colour search over the whole palette. The one
   exception is the water, which can reflect anything, so it may use a 21-colour subset.
4. **Clusters.** In-between values are laid with the material's own threshold map (1.6):
   * **sky**: flat bands with checker seams (`w=0.32`);
   * **near grass**: Ferrari's measured recipe, **base 38% / mid stroke 35% / olive 11% / gap 12%**
     in light and **black 28% / black-green 31% / olive 40%** in shade, thresholded on a
     vertically stretched noise so the proportions come out as vertical 1-px strokes;
   * **far grass**: a two-colour speckle;
   * **rock**: a value spread along diagonal fall lines, with checker between value zones;
   * **water**: horizontal ripple noise, with checker only in the middle of a blend;
   * **conifers**: black and violet-black with ochre flecks on the sun side of each drooping tier
     (black, ochre, dark: 3 colours).
5. **The boulder's light plan.** It is painted as a painter plans it:
   * one clean terminator from the *big* form (the undisplaced ellipsoid's normal);
   * zones of highlight, light and half-tone on the lit side;
   * core, shadow and sky-lit top on the shadow side;
   * a one-step darkening where the lit form turns away from us;
   * facets, with a light line on the sun side of a facet edge and a dark crease on the other.

   The lumps only move values inside zones.
6. **Pixel geometry.** From 1.9 and David's note. The boulder silhouette, its reflection and the
   bank silhouettes are simplified to polygons. Every edge is snapped to a clean pixel slope (flat,
   5:1 … 1:1 … 1:4, vertical), a least-squares solve finds vertex positions that keep the shape
   closed, and the result is re-rasterised. The mountain crest is a polyline of clean-slope
   segments. **Every mass that meets water gets a flat, 1-px dark contact line**, and the boulder
   gets a flat bottom. The stones in the shallows are flat lozenges with a light top row, mid body,
   dark bottom row and 45° ends.
7. **Strokes: impressionistic contrast inside the stroke.** Grass blades are straight runs with
   clean steps: a lower segment at one slope and the tip at a shallower one. They are painted back
   to front in counterchange:
   * on light ground, a dark blade **with its lit edge beside it**;
   * on dark ground, a light blade that gets lighter toward the tip, **with a dark shadow side**.

   Sedge rises around the boulder's foot and along the waterline, where it breaks the lost edge
   between boulder and bank. The blades at the waterline get short, broken dark reflections.
8. **Reflection.** An exact mirror (the ray is reflected in the water plane and traced again,
   including the boulder, banks and conifers). The calibrated transform: the sky loses ~4 L\*, top
   lights lose ~13, darks stay put, and a\* drops 4. Water reflects slightly less toward the frame
   bottom. There are sparse 1-px streak lines, denser toward the far shore: the rows Ferrari would
   colour-cycle.
9. **Light direction.** It changes more than shading. Each direction carries a scene decision
   taken from the reference:
   * the sky's warm side follows the sun;
   * front light gives a **Belt of Venus** (the pink band above the violet Earth-shadow);
   * back light gives a glow above the frame, rim-lit boulder, trees and blade tips, a silhouetted
   peak, and a whole foreground in shade;
   * side light puts the far side of the near V in the shadow of off-frame trees (the reference's
     dark right bank);
   * grass counts as lit whenever the sun reaches it, because blades stand upright and catch a
     grazing sun whatever the slope of the ground.

### The palette (32 colours, chosen per material)

| ramp | colours |
|---|---|
| sky (iso-luminant hue sweep) | sky0 violet, sky1, sky2 pink, sky3 salmon, sky4 peach, hot |
| distant rock (lifted darks) | mtn0 L37 violet, mtn1, mtn2 alpenglow salmon, snow0 lavender |
| far forest | for0 violet-black, for1 |
| grass, lit (warm) | g0 L11 black-green, g1 L24 olive, g2 L40 brown, g3 L51, g4 L60 ochre, g5 L73 back-lit tip |
| grass, shade | k black, g0, h1, g1, h2 olive; sedge (greener) |
| granite | r0 core L15, r1 shadow violet-grey, r2 half-tone mauve, r3 warm light, r4 peach highlight |
| water | w0 bed/deep, w1, w2, w3 (reflected sky, cooled), glint |
EOF
echo ok

"""Comparison plates: the same analyses on Ferrari and on painted cells, side by side."""
import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'src'); from util import *
from scipy.ndimage import gaussian_filter
from atlas import atlas
from edges import draw as edge_draw
CELLS='sheets/cells'
def notan_img(a, cuts=(28.9,55.6)):
    Lb=gaussian_filter(lum(a),2.5*a.shape[1]/634)   # same squint relative to picture width
    q=np.digitize(Lb,cuts); lv=np.array([25,130,240]); g=lv[q].astype(np.uint8)
    return np.stack([g]*3,-1)
def fit(a,w):
    return np.array(Image.fromarray(a).resize((w,int(a.shape[0]*w/a.shape[1])),Image.NEAREST))
ref=load_ref()
keys=['medium_left_10m_s1','medium_front_10m_s1','medium_back_10m_s1','medium_left_close_s1','medium_left_40m_s1','medium_left_10m_s4']
W0=420
tiles=[label(fit(ref,W0),'Ferrari'),label(fit(notan_img(ref),W0),'Ferrari 3-notan')]
for k in keys:
    a=np.array(Image.open(f'{CELLS}/{k}.png').convert('RGB'))
    tiles+= [label(fit(a,W0),k),label(fit(notan_img(a),W0),'3-notan (same cuts)')]
rows=[]
for i in range(0,len(tiles),4):
    r=tiles[i:i+4]
    h=max(t.shape[0] for t in r)
    r=[np.pad(t,((0,h-t.shape[0]),(0,6),(0,0)),constant_values=255) for t in r]
    while len(r)<4: r.append(np.full_like(r[0],255))
    rows.append(np.pad(np.concatenate(r,1),((0,6),(0,0),(0,0)),constant_values=255))
Image.fromarray(np.concatenate(rows,0)).save('img/20_notan_compare.png')
# cluster atlas for my base cell (same windows by material)
a=np.array(Image.open(f'{CELLS}/medium_left_10m_s1.png').convert('RGB'))
P=np.load(f'{CELLS}/medium_left_10m_s1_planes.npy')
def win(pl,cond=None):
    ys,xs=np.nonzero(P==pl)
    ok=(ys>2)&(ys<a.shape[0]-34)&(xs<a.shape[1]-34)
    if cond is not None: ok&=cond(ys,xs)
    i=np.flatnonzero(ok)
    j=i[len(i)//2] if len(i) else 0
    return (int(xs[j]),int(ys[j]))
wins=[('near grass, lit',win(6,lambda y,x: x<120)),('near grass, shade',win(6,lambda y,x: x>220)),('bank by boulder',win(4)),
      ('boulder',win(5)),('grove (conifer)',win(8)),('water',win(3,lambda y,x:(y>120)&(x>120)&(x<200))),
      ('far forest',win(2)),('mountain',win(1)),('sky',win(0))]
atlas(a,[(t,(max(0,x-4),max(0,y-4))) for t,(x,y) in wins],'21_cluster_atlas_mine.png',s=7,sz=32)
edge_draw(a,'22_edges_mine',3)
EOF
sed -i '' "s|    save(out.astype(np.uint8),f'{prefix}_edges.png',scale)|    save(out.astype(np.uint8),f'{prefix}_edges.png' if not prefix.endswith('mine') else f'{prefix}.png',scale)|" src/edges.py
nice -n 10 python3 src/compare_plates.py && ls img | grep -E "^2[0-2]"

## 4. Checking the sheets with the same analyses (`src/check_sheets.py`, `src/compare_plates.py`)

I ran the value, notan, cluster, dither, palette-coverage and depth code from section 1 unchanged on
all 19 rendered cells, using the planes the painter records. The table gives the median over the
cells, then Ferrari's value for the corresponding plane (`out/check_summary.json`,
`out/check_all.txt`). Squint L = median of the σ=2.5 blurred L\*. "v-same" and "h-same" = share of
vertical and horizontal neighbours that are the same colour. "cols 90%" = colours covering 90% of
the plane.

| my plane ↔ Ferrari plane | squint L | p5–p95 L | cols 90% | v-same / h-same | mean cluster | checker share | b\* |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| near bank, shade ↔ right bank | **15.7 / 14.2** | **1–24 / 0–26** | **4 / 4** | **0.67 / 0.68**, 0.31 / 0.36 | **5.2 / 5.6** | 0.02 / 0.04 | **13 / 14** |
| near bank, lit ↔ left bank | 39.2 / 43.4 | **11–61 / 11–60** | **4 / 4** | 0.58 / 0.70, 0.26 / 0.34 | 3.5 / 6.2 | 0.03 / 0.05 | **34 / 35** |
| bank by boulder ↔ left meadow | **40.3 / 41.9** | **11–61 / 11–61** | 6 / 7 | **0.55 / 0.56**, 0.38 / 0.44 | 3.6 / 4.5 | 0.03 / 0.06 | 34 / 27 |
| grove ↔ framing trees | 12.0 / 15.6 | **1–40 / 0–45** | **3 / 3** | **0.51 / 0.56**, 0.59 / 0.52 | **4.6 / 5.2** | 0.03 / 0.07 | **1 / 0** |
| far forest ↔ far forest | **20.6 / 19.8** | 1–40 / 0–45 | 4 / 10 | 0.31 / 0.46, 0.62 / 0.45 | 4.1 / 3.8 | 0.01 / 0.06 | 1 / 0 |
| mountain ↔ mountain | 56.6 / 48.4 | **37–75 / 36–77** | 5 / 15 | 0.55 / 0.42 | 5.3 / 3.3 | 0.05 / 0.10 | −7 / −5 |
| boulder ↔ mountain rock | 32.1 / 48.4 | 14–65 / 36–77 | 4 / 15 | 0.75 / 0.42 | **10.7 / 3.3** | 0.04 / 0.10 | −8 / −5 |
| water ↔ water | **59.0 / 29.0** | 1–71 / 0–72 | 12 / 69 | 0.37 / 0.33, 0.49 / 0.33 | 2.5 / 2.3 | **0.27 / 0.08** | −8 / +2 |
| sky ↔ sky | 64.9 / 71.4 | 73–75 / 71–75 | 2 / 8 | — | — | 0.08 / 0.25 | 3 / 1 |
| *area share* | water **54%** vs 17%; sky 1% vs 18%; near banks 17% vs 24% | | | | | | |

Bold = a close match.

### Analysis by analysis

**Planes and masses** (`sheets/*`, `img/20_notan_compare.png`). The structure transferred:

* a V of foreground banks opening onto water, lit on one side and dark on the other;
* a promontory carrying the subject;
* dark framing conifers;
* a peak whose reflection sits inside the V.

The proportions did not transfer. **The water takes 54% of my frame against 17%**, and the sky 1%
against 18%. The cause is the task's camera. Looking 10° down with a 29° vertical field puts the
horizon at row 29 of 180, so most of the frame *has* to be ground or water. I leaned into it:
peak and trees taller than the frame, shown whole in the water. But the picture is a different
shape from Ferrari's, whose camera looks level and gives the sky and the trees half the picture.

**Values and notan** (`img/20_notan_compare.png`, `img/chk_*_grey_vs.png`, `img/chk_*_valueplanes.png`).
With Ferrari's own notan cuts, both pictures read as an hourglass: dark masses left and right, and
a light shape pouring down the middle. The balance is inverted, though. **Ferrari's picture is
about 60% dark with a light top. Mine is light-dominant, with a dark top band** (far forest plus
crowns) and a large light water field. Water squint L is 59 against 29, because his water mostly
mirrors tall black trees and mine mostly mirrors sky. The planes themselves sit in the right value
groups. Near shade 15.7 vs 14.2. Lit meadow 40 vs 42. The grove's darks at black. The peak's
darks lifted to L37 (exactly Ferrari's 36). Nothing white: my sky tops out at L75 and only the
back-lit rim and the snow reach L80. **The foreground value groups are the best transfer in the
study. The overall notan balance is the biggest miss.** The lit near bank still runs 4 L too dark:
my lit/shade transition is made of strokes, and some of them are shade.

**Light** (`sheets/sheet_light.png`). One rule, visible in every cell: warm light and cool shade,
per material. Lit ochre against black-olive, peach granite against violet, salmon rock against
violet rock, lit snow against lavender. The four directions each make a different picture, not
just different shading:

* **left and right**: the far side of the V goes into off-frame shadow;
* **front**: the Belt of Venus, the lit boulder front, and the mountain's lit faces turning warm;
* **back**: glow, a rim-lit boulder with a one-pixel peach halo, rim-lit trees, a silhouetted
  violet peak, and the whole foreground in shade.

Front light is my weakest. With the sun behind the viewer, the boulder has little shadow and
reads as a pale mass. A painter would push the terminator and core harder than physics does.

**Form** (the close cells in `sheet_distance.png` and `sheet_light_x_distance.png`). The boulder
has what the form analysis asked for: one clean terminator from the big form, highlight, light and
half-tone zones, a core shadow, a sky-lit shadow top, a flat water contact with a dark lip, and a
reflection with the lit side reading warm. It is **smoother than Ferrari's rock**: mean cluster 10.7
against 3.3, and 4 colours against 15. His rock is a distant mountain made of many small planes
and diagonal strokes. My boulder is one big close form in a 5-colour granite ramp. At 10 m and
40 m it reads as a boulder. At 4.5 m the lumps give it lobes, but not yet the crisp faceted planes
I set out to paint. Ferrari's lozenge hummock (lit rim, dark face) appears in my far-right bank,
but it is only a thin strip at this camera angle.

**Edges** (`img/22_edges_mine.png` against `img/08_edges.png`). The same distribution. The hard
edges are silhouettes against light water: the boulder's reflection, the conifers' reflections,
and the V banks against the water. The soft edges are the grass interiors. The lost edges are dark
against dark: the grove's crowns against the far forest, the boulder's shadow side into its own
reflection (joined only by the 1-px lip), and the bank into the boulder foot, where sedge breaks it.
I did not reproduce the *sky* edges, because there is almost no sky.

**Clusters** (`img/21_cluster_atlas_mine.png` against `img/09_cluster_atlas.png`; `out/cmp_grass.png`
puts my near lit grass beside his at 6×).

* **Near shade grass** is effectively Ferrari's: black and black-green ground with olive strokes, 4
  colours, vertical coherence 0.67 vs 0.68, mean cluster 5.2 vs 5.6. That came straight from
  transcribing his measured proportions.
* **Near lit grass** has the right colours, proportions and warmth (b\* 34 vs 35), but its strokes
  are shorter: mean cluster 3.5 vs 6.2, vertical coherence 0.58 vs 0.70. The paired
  light-and-dark blades add horizontal alternation that he doesn't have at that density.
* **Conifers** match on colour count, cluster size and single-pixel share. But **their reflections
  are too regular**: the tiers repeat as even chevrons, where his are broken and noisy.
* **Water** has **too much checkerboard** (27% of 2×2 blocks against 8%). My reflected sky is a
  large flat area that needs a blend pattern. His reflected sky is small and cut by reflections.
* **Sky** and **far forest**: I have too few colours to judge the sky (2 visible bands). The forest
  flecks read as horizontal strokes (h-same 0.62 vs 0.45), where his are more speckled.

**Palette** (`src/paint/ferrari_method.py` PAL). 32 colours as material ramps, with the same two
hue families (sun ochres and peaches against sky violets and pinks). Chroma peaks mid-value. There
is a pure black, a lifted violet ramp for distance and an iso-luminant sky sweep. The budget is
the obvious difference from 172 colours. I spent it where the analysis said the picture's
structure lives (foreground ramps at 4 colours each, granite at 5, sky at 6). That starves the
mountain (5 colours used, against 15) and the water (12 against 69).

**Depth** (`img/chk_*_depth.png` against `img/12_depth.png`):

* **Overlap**: near V, promontory, boulder, groves, far forest, peak.
* **The black point rises**: black near and in the forest, L37 at the peak, as in the reference.
* **Hue**: ochre near, violet far.
* **Texture scale**: strokes near, speckle at the promontory at 40 m.

All four cues are present and in the right order. What's missing is Ferrari's middle-distance
chroma dip. My promontory is as saturated as the foreground (chroma 34).

**Pixel geometry** (David's note). Silhouettes are clean-slope polygons, and every mass has a
flat base and a dark contact line at the water. The crest is a clean polyline, stones are
lozenges, and blades are straight runs with one change of slope. At native size this is the
biggest single improvement between my first and last sheets: shapes look drawn rather than
sampled. The grammar is still applied as a post-process, though. The boulder's big silhouette
sometimes snaps into over-long straight edges, a slightly boxy octagon, where Ferrari varies his
run lengths along a curve.

---

## 5. What I would do next

1. **Rebalance the notan to Ferrari's roughly 60% dark.** Let the grove crowns and the far forest
   occupy more of the top, and give the conifer reflections more mass and noise. Or, a question
   for the task's owner: let the camera rise to near-level so the sky and tree masses get their
   share. The 10° camera is the biggest reason my pictures are shaped differently from his.
2. **Break the conifer reflections** with per-tier jitter, sky holes, and a speckle-dissolve
   toward the tips, as his do.
3. **Facet the close boulder properly.** Build it as a few large planes carved by fracture lines,
   each plane one value with checker seams, rather than a lumpy ellipsoid nudged by Voronoi facets.
4. **Lengthen the lit-grass strokes.** Draw the lit-edge pairs only on a minority of blades, and
   bring the lit bank's value up 4 L.
5. **Colour cycling.** Reserve 3–4 palette slots as a cycling ramp for the water streak rows and
   shallows, and export an animated GIF per cell. That is the part of Ferrari's craft this study
   only describes.
